K travels in the request under D-140, so a demo default is not a specification change, and FR-SIM-10 requires the response to be independent of it — D-369's sweep already covers K ∈ {1, 7, 250, 100000} on all three x86 backends. 200 frames is about ten seconds at 20 fps |
| D-377 | **P-21 is approved as drafted and becomes the plan of record for Phase G Stages 2, 3 and 4** — `docs/plan/2026-09-16-phase-g-stages-2-3-4.md`. Stage 5 stays out of scope under D-374 | Approve Stage 2 alone and re-approve the geometry sidecar and viewer once the stream had been seen working, which is safer but likely ends the session before anything is watchable; discuss the stream record, the sparse encoding or the panel layout before any of it is written | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16. D-366 approved Stage 1 alone and left Stages 2 to 5 as proposals *"to be approved individually when reached"*; this is Stages 2 to 4 being reached. The plan's load-bearing property is that the kernel is not touched: the stream is differenced from `st->spikes[]` and read from `st->u[]` by the driver between `onfcont` calls, so FR-SIM-07 stays true of the core |
| D-378 | **The chunk size K reaches the driver as a PARM keyword, `PARM='STREAM=50'`.** The request record is not changed | Put K in the request record, which is literally what D-140 says: the record grows past 412 bytes, the generated COBOL copybook, C header and Python struct all move, every recorded MVS artefact goes stale, and TX-01's measured 412-byte identity (D-261) must be re-established on TK5; a new positional argument, zero layout cost but invisible to MVS JCL, which passes PARM and DD names rather than `argv` | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16, with the cost of the literal reading stated before the choice. **This is a narrower reading of D-140 than its words** — K becomes per *run* rather than per *record* — and it is recorded as such rather than as an implementation detail. PARM is what MVS JCL actually passes, so Stage 5 inherits the mechanism unchanged |
| D-379 | **The stream is written to its own file, `argv[5]`, defaulting to `DD:ONFSTM`** — not to stdout | stdout, interleaved with the ONF operator messages: one fewer file to allocate and the viewer could read a pipe directly | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16. The report and the numeric stream would otherwise share a channel, and on MVS that channel is SYSPRINT, which carries the report. A separate DD maps onto Stage 5 directly. The stream is flushed after every chunk, so a viewer tailing the file follows the writer rather than reading a finished recording — which is the replay D-128 rejected |
| D-380 | **Both guards join `make test`:** the Section 8.4 golden fingerprints re-checked with streaming on, and a stream-versus-response agreement test | The fingerprint guard only, leaving stream-versus-response as a tool run by hand; neither, keeping `make test` at its present cost and evidencing both by explicit runs in the session that writes them | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16. The distinction was stated before the choice: the fingerprint guard proves the *response* did not change, and only the agreement test proves the stream describes the **same run** rather than a plausible animation beside it. Both reuse builds the suite already makes |
| D-381 | **Appendix E gains two messages: `ONF907S STREAM DATASET UNWRITABLE` and `ONF908S PARM INVALID: text`**, both severity S at RC 16 | Add `ONF907S` only, so a mistyped PARM is reported as an unwritable dataset — which is false; add neither and report both with the existing `ONF906S`, whose text names the *request* dataset and so is false in both cases | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16. Appendix E had no message for either failure and the nearest one names a different dataset. IR-MSG-01's form `ONFnnns` is satisfied by both, and 907/908 continue the 9xx severe block that 901..906 already occupy |
| D-382 | **The chunk stream becomes a specified interface: SRS Section 4.7 gains IR-STM-01 to IR-STM-04, and Section 8.5 gains TU-11.** An authorised addition of **new requirement text** | A single IR-STM-01 saying only that a stream may be emitted and must not affect the response, leaving the record shape to the implementation document; no new requirements at all, on the ground that D-139 and D-140 already fix what is emitted and by what mechanism | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16. PARM is already a specified interface — IR-TRN-03 fixes `PARM='VERIFY'` with a verification method — so a second PARM value with nothing in Section 4 behind it would be the only undocumented entry point in the engine. The record shape is what Stage 5 and any later consumer must agree on, and an implementation document is not a contract |
| D-383 | **`engine/src/onfreq.c` is split into `onfrqb`, `onfrqc` and `onfrqe`, with `onfrq1k` redefined as exactly their composition** — D-366's own pattern applied one level up, so the streaming driver runs the same code path by construction rather than by test | Give `onfrq1k` an observer function pointer that the driver supplies: a smaller API change, but function pointers appear nowhere in the engine today and have been compiled by neither GCCMVS nor JCC, and no MVS run was available this session to prove them; let the driver call `onfinit`/`onfcont` itself and repeat the validation, the step count and the fingerprint | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-16. The third option is the exact condition that produced D-289 and then D-360 — one criterion with two implementations, drifting silently — and D-224 put this sequence in one place deliberately. Three new external names, `onfrqb`, `onfrqc`, `onfrqe`, six characters each and distinct ignoring case from `onfrqg`, `onfrqp`, `onfrq1` and `onfrq1k` (C-04) |
| D-384 | **The 14 stimulus neurons are placed at the centroid of their own postsynaptic targets within the network, and stay flagged `placed`.** They carry no `somaLocation` at all — every one of them — and they are 14 of the 19 unplaced bodies | Keep the subcircuit centroid, which is what `prep/geom.py` did when first written: all 14 on one point at z = 90,408, collapsing the whole stimulus set into a single dot and putting it deep in the nerve cord; place them at a marked position outside the CNS bounding box, honest about the cell bodies being peripheral but carrying no per-neuron information | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17. **Their absence is biology, not a data gap:** sugar-sensing GRNs (PhG9, dorsal_tpGRN — D-52) have peripheral cell bodies in the proboscis, outside the imaged CNS volume, so no `somaLocation` exists to find. Measured before the choice: each has 15 to 23 targets with measured positions, and the centroids land in the SEZ — dorsal_tpGRN at z ≈ 19,000, PhG9 at z ≈ 26,000 to 30,000, separated left and right on x — beside the MN9 readouts at z ≈ 15,500, which is where GRN axon terminals are. The `placed` flag is kept so the viewer can draw them as imputed; a position that is inferred is never presented as measured |
| D-385 | **`data/geom/srext-geom.json` (126 KB) and `data/geom/backdrop.csv` (496 KB, 20,255 of 141,781 somata, every 7th by bodyId order) are both committed**, so the viewer runs from a clean checkout without the 14.5 MB annotations feather | Halve the backdrop to about 10,000 points and 250 KB, visibly sparser where somata are dense; commit only the sidecar and regenerate the cloud, which keeps the repository smallest but makes the viewer need the fixture before it draws anything | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17, with both sizes measured first. The stride is deterministic over bodyId-sorted order rather than a random sample, so two runs are byte-identical — the property FR-PRP-09 requires of the prep pipeline, and verified in this session |
| D-386 | **This session commits once per stage and pushes the branch to `origin` when the work is verified.** No pull request is opened and nothing on `main` moves | One commit at the end for Stages 2 to 4 together, simpler history but leaving the earlier stages uncommitted if the session fails late; commit and rebase onto `main` immediately as D-372 did for Stage 1, so `main` is never behind the real state | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17. The /goal requires everything pushed while `.claude/skills/onfly-engineer/SKILL.md` says never to push, so the instruction is taken in this session as it was in D-359 and D-372; the owner's instruction is the more specific of the two |
| D-387 | **The live viewer's raster orders its rows down the measured body axis, brain at top — not by neuron index.** `--order index` keeps the other behaviour. **An authorised deviation from P-21**, which specified *"Neuron index against time"* | Keep neuron index exactly as P-21 drafted it, reproducible from the network file alone with no geometry involved; ship the `--order` switch with index as the default and let whoever records a clip choose, deviating from nothing but making the demo depend on a flag being passed | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17, after seeing both rendered. Neuron index is an artefact of SR-EXT-03's extraction ordering and means nothing anatomically; sorted down the body axis the same spikes read as a wave crossing the animal. The order uses the axis `prep/geom.py` measured (D-384's projection block), so it inherits that measurement rather than assuming one |
| D-388 | **Two clips are recorded, not one:** a 60 ms window at K=5 as the explainer, and the full 1000 ms standard run at K=50 as the evidence that the explainer is a real standard request | The short window alone, which is the legible one but invites the question of whether the demo quietly shrank the problem; the full standard run alone, which is the D-73 duration and the D-376 chunk size but whose raster reads as a solid block | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17. **Measured before the choice, and it is why the block is honest:** at 40 Hz an average of 209 of 501 neurons fire in every 5 ms chunk (max 256; 419 distinct neurons over the run), so the shipped subcircuit is densely active under sugar and MN9's 27 spikes are a rare event inside it. The short window shows silence, arrival, propagation and MN9 crossing threshold at 25.8 ms — the same latency the response record carries |
| D-389 | **The recorded clips are committed under `docs/media/`** | Hand them over and leave them out of the repository, on the argument that a render of a reproducible run adds weight without adding truth | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17, with the cost stated: binary in git history that goes stale whenever the rendering changes. The reviewer who cannot build the engine is the reason |
| D-390 | **`tests/test_varnt.py`'s skip guard is corrected to name every MaleCNS feather it actually reads — the annotations file *and* the weights file** | Provision the 1.05 GB weights feather into this worktree and every future one, which changes no code and makes the test really run rather than skip; do both; log the defect and delete the annotations feather again, restoring the skip but leaving `prep/geom.py` unrunnable here and the next person to hit the same crash | Chosen by the owner through AskUserQuestion on 2026-09-17. The guard skipped on `body-annotations-...feather` while the test reaches the 1.05 GB `connectome-weights-...feather` through `calibrate.load_cache` → `signs.build_signs`. It was latent until this session: D-384's `prep/geom.py` needs the annotations file, so `tools/fixtures.py --malecns annotations` became a normal thing to run, and provisioning one feather without the other turned a skip into a `FileNotFoundError` that failed `make test` at the `prep` target. Found outside the session's chosen scope and asked rather than fixed, as D-360 was |

### A.2 Design decisions proposed in this draft

These were introduced by the architect while writing the specification. They are subject to owner review and are listed separately so that proposals are never mistaken for owner decisions.

| ID | Proposal | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| P-01 | Responses carry numeric readout identifiers; the fingerprint excludes text fields (IR-COM-05, IR-COM-06) | Text differs between ASCII and EBCDIC hosts; numbers do not |
| P-02 | Integer-only stimulus draws with rejection sampling (NR-12) | Exactly uniform and needs no floating point |
| P-03 | Subnormal clamp on the synaptic term (NR-08) | Removes the one place where host floating-point modes could differ |
| P-04 | One linear update kernel for both integration methods (Appendix C) | The method changes constants, not code |
| P-05 | Absolute tolerance floors alongside relative tolerances (ACC-3, ACC-4) | Relative tolerances become meaningless near zero firing rates |
| P-06 | Verify-only mode (IR-TRN-03) | Makes transport spikes and operational checks cheap |
| ~~P-07~~ | Source line length on MVS | **CLOSED 2026-09-11 by D-93** — measured: the TK5 card reader truncates at 80 columns silently. ONFLY sources are held to 80 columns and linted; vendored `third_party/` is exempt and needs a different transport |
| P-08 | Add an x86-64 / SOFT2C row to the Section 8.3 determinism matrix | D-124 renamed the matrix's backend values but did not add rows, so ACC-5 is unchanged. x86 can run both soft libraries, and the 2c backend's golden fingerprints on x86 are what make a later MVS fingerprint meaningful — an MVS-only 2c row would have nothing on the same library to be compared against. Proposed, not decided; ACC-5's row set is the owner's to change |
| ~~P-09~~ | Add a 60 Hz validation-rate request (SUGR, 60 Hz, standard duration, seed 1) to the golden suite of Section 8.4, so that every TBD-07 validation rate has a golden fingerprint | **CLOSED 2026-09-14 by D-212 — adopted as written and entered as G-14.** The reason stood unchanged: D-165 added 60 Hz to the validation set after the suite was drafted, and ACC-5 is evaluated on the suite, so the rate had no determinism evidence |
| ~~P-10~~ | In SR-CAL-03's objective, the relative error at a calibration rate is `\|onfly - ref\| / max(ref, 2 Hz)`, 2 Hz being ACC-4's floor (D-166); `prep/calibrate.py` records at which rates the floor was applied | **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-12 by D-172** — the reference at 20 Hz turned out to be exactly zero, the owner chose to exclude zero-reference rates from the objective, and no divisor floor is used |
| P-11 | ACC-4's shape clause, "does not decrease between successive validation rates by more than one standard error", is evaluated with the standard error of ONFLY's own mean at the earlier rate, `sd / sqrt(30)` over the D-135 seeds; `prep/acc4.py` implements this reading | ACC-4 does not say whose standard error. Proposed, not decided; the evaluation output labels every shape verdict with this interpretation |
| P-12 | Section 9.2's Phase A row reads "G0; spikes S1–S4 — **S1 (G1), S2 (G2), S4 (G4) closed; S3 (G3) measured**", which now under-states the phase: G0 is closed as of D-241 and the row does not say so. The proposal is to annotate it "**G0 closed 2026-09-14 (D-241)**; S1 (G1), S2 (G2), S4 (G4) closed; S3 (G3) measured", making Phase A readable as complete | D-240 authorised exactly two text changes — the Section 9.1 G0 row and the Section 2.6 A-03/A-04 cells — and the Phase A row is neither, so this stays a proposal rather than being made on the grounds that it is obviously implied. Proposed, not decided |
| P-13 | **`data/networks/MANIFEST.json`'s `srext.acceptance.not_proven` field is now false in three places and should be corrected.** It reads *"Nothing is proven for Linux s390x, MVS 3.8j or z/OS; the v1.1 header has never been decoded by GCCMVS. ... ACC-5, ACC-6 and ACC-7 are unevaluated on it."* Phase D proved s390x on 2026-09-14 (D-234), and on 2026-09-15 GCCMVS decoded the v1.1 header on TK5 — `ONF001I NETWORK LOADED N=501 E=10783`, `ONF002I BIAS ROWS 9` — and ACC-5 was evaluated on it and passed for all five `srext` golden requests (VL-91). ACC-6 and ACC-7 remain genuinely unevaluated. | **Why this is a proposal and not a fix:** the field is written by `prep/emit.py`, and D-269 established that re-running that tool rewrites `data/networks/*.bin` and would invalidate every Section 8.4 fingerprint — so correcting it means either editing a generated manifest by hand or giving `prep/manifest.py` a way to amend an acceptance note without re-emitting. Both are owner decisions. Left in A.2 until the owner rules |
| ~~P-14~~ | **Section 2.6's A-04 row should record what JCC predefines, not only what it does not.** It currently says only *"it defines no `__VERSION__`"*. Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-15 under D-291: of nineteen candidates probed, JCC predefines exactly **two** — `JCC` = `1` and `__STDC__` = `1` — and defines none of `__JCC__`, `__LCC__`, `__lcc__`, `__MVS__`, `__CMS__`, `__370__`, `__S370__`, `__s390__`, `__s390x__`, `__IBMC__`, `__GNUC__`, `__STDC_VERSION__`, `MVS`, `_MVS`, `__EBCDIC__`, `__BIG_ENDIAN__` or `__TOS_MVS__`. The proposal is to add that sentence to the A-04 cell | A-04 is an assumption row that now carries a measurement, and the measurement is load-bearing: `engine/src/onflyeng.c` and `engine/include/onfplat.h` both key a branch on `JCC` **because** it is the only macro available, and `ONF_PLATID`'s branch infers the platform from a compiler macro for the same reason. A future reader who does not know the probe result cannot tell why those branches are shaped that way. **CLOSED 2026-09-15 by D-299 — adopted as written.** The reason stood: D-291 authorised the code branches, not a change to Section 2.6's text, so it stayed a proposal until the owner said otherwise. The A-04 cell now carries the measurement |
| ~~P-15~~ | **A requirement ID for TBD-19's carried obligation, proposed as FR-SIM-10:** *"When a request is executed in chunks (D-140), the response shall be identical to the response of the same request executed in a single chunk, for every chunk size K ≥ 1 and for every placement of chunk boundaries. In particular the fingerprint of IR-COM-05 shall not depend on K. Verification: T — tests/test_chunk.py at the oracle level; in Phase G, the engine's chunked entry point against the Section 8.4 golden suite."* | TBD-19 closed recording that this *"needs a requirement ID and a test before Phase G is built"*, and D-316 authorised proposing the ID. **The test exists already** (D-317, VL-107) and passes, so what is proposed here is only the wording that gives the property a name the test catalogue and Section 8.5 can cite. Placed in FR-SIM because it constrains the simulation contract rather than the record layout, though it is IR-COM-05's fingerprint that it protects. **CLOSED 2026-09-16 by D-318 — adopted as written and entered as FR-SIM-10, with TU-10 added to Section 8.5.** The reason stood unchanged: the test existed and passed, and what was missing was only the name the catalogue could cite |
| ~~P-16~~ | **The ACC-4 replacement text D-340 authorises, for the owner to approve before it is written into Section 6.4:** *"**ACC-4 — Agreement with Shiu.** **Shape (the criterion).** At the validation rates, the MN9 rate shall not decrease between successive rates by more than one standard error, and the onset rate — the lowest validation rate with mean above 1 Hz — shall be within one sampled rate of the reference's onset. ACC-4 passes when both hold. **Magnitude (reported, not tested).** Each validation point's deviation from the Shiu reference shall be reported with every ACC-4 result, against a tolerance of ±25% or an absolute floor of ±2 Hz (D-166), whichever is larger, naming every rate that falls outside it. A magnitude deviation shall not, by itself, fail ACC-4. (Amended 2026-09-16 by D-340 on the grounds of VL-06: the reference is a model calibrated on a female connectome and ONFLY runs a male one, so agreement shows consistency rather than identity. VL-103, VL-108 and VL-109 measured that no W_syn satisfies the magnitude clause for any stimulus set considered — the shipped D-52 set, the dorsal_tpGRN variant or the unilateral right variant — because the dose-response curves differ in shape and W_syn is a single scalar.)"* | **What this changes, stated plainly rather than buried:** ACC-4 would pass on evidence that has not moved, because the shape clause already passed and the magnitude clause already failed. The bar is lower than it was. What is kept is the reporting obligation — the deviations at 10 and 40 Hz remain on the record with every result, so nothing is hidden by the change, only reclassified. SR-CAL-05 is untouched. **CLOSED 2026-09-16 by D-341 — approved as drafted and written into Section 6.4.** `prep/acc4.py` was changed with it, so the tool's own verdict follows the amended criterion rather than the reader being asked to reinterpret a `FAIL` |
| ~~P-17~~ | **APPROVED 2026-09-16 by D-366 as the plan of record.** Stage 1 is authorised exactly as drafted; Stages 2 to 5 remain proposals until each is approved on its own. **The Phase G live-view plan, drafted for approval before any code is written.** Five stages: (1) a resumable kernel -- `onfinit` and `onfcont`, with `onfrun` redefined as their composition so that bit-identity is structural rather than tested for afterwards; (2) the D-139 stream emitted entirely in the driver by differencing `st->spikes[]` between chunks, so the kernel keeps FR-SIM-07 and the MVS engine needs no kernel change either; (3) a geometry sidecar `prep/geom.py` joining `SUBCIRCUIT.json` to the annotations feather, flagging the 19 neurons without a `somaLocation` as placed rather than inventing positions silently; (4) the three-panel viewer of D-345; (5) the MVS half last. Written in full at `docs/plan/2026-09-16-phase-g-live-view.md` | The owner asked on 2026-09-16 that this work be planned ahead rather than started, and answered the three design questions (D-344 to D-346) while leaving the plan's shape for approval. Stage 1 is the only change to the simulation contract and is precisely what FR-SIM-10 still calls "open against the engine"; every later stage is gated behind the 19 Section 8.4 golden fingerprints being unchanged, because a chunk-dependent fingerprint would cost the determinism claim rather than a feature |
| ~~P-18~~ | **ADOPTED 2026-09-16 by D-357 and written into Section 6.4's ACC-3.** The ACC-3 reporting clause D-354 authorised, as drafted for approval. Drafted in two sentences so that the second can be dropped without touching the first. *"Every ACC-3 result shall report, for each tested rate, the standard error of the full-brain reference mean over the seeds used, alongside the measured difference and the tolerance. Where a rate's margin -- the tolerance less the absolute difference -- is smaller than that standard error, the result shall name that rate as lying within the reference's own precision."* The first sentence is exactly what D-354 authorises; the second is an addition by the engineer, offered because a number printed beside a verdict is easily read past, while a named rate is not. | At 40 Hz the margin is 0.56 Hz and the reference mean's standard error at 30 seeds is 1.5425 Hz (VL-114), so a PASS printed without that figure reads more precise than the comparison can be. Follows D-202, which excludes and reports a rate the reference cannot pin down, and D-341, which made ACC-4's magnitude a reported quantity rather than a test: in both the project chose to state what the criterion cannot establish instead of narrowing the criterion until it could |
| ~~P-19~~ | **ADOPTED 2026-09-16 by D-371 and written into Section 3.3.** The replacement status note for FR-SIM-10, as drafted for approval. The requirement's own sentences are NOT touched — only the parenthetical status note after them, which currently asserts something that stopped being true on 2026-09-16. It reads today: *"**Met by the oracle and open against the engine**: `engine/src/onfker.c` has no chunked entry point, so Phase G must show this against the Section 8.4 golden suite — VL-107."* Proposed replacement: *"**Met by the oracle (VL-107) and, since 2026-09-16, by the engine (VL-115).** `engine/src/onfker.c` gained `onfinit` and `onfcont` under D-366, with `onfrun` defined as their composition; `engine/src/onfreq.c` gained `onfrq1k` under D-368. All nineteen Section 8.4 requests were driven at K ∈ {1, 7, 250, 100000} on x86-64 across SOFT3E, SOFT2C and NATIVE, and every GOLD and GOUT line was byte-identical to the unchunked run (D-369). **x86-64 only**: no chunked run has been made on Linux s390x, MVS 3.8j or z/OS."* | The existing note states as present fact something that a session has since changed, which is the one kind of staleness a specification cannot carry: a reader would conclude the engine still cannot chunk. The replacement keeps the same shape — what is met, by what evidence, and what is not proven — and adds the platform limit the old note had no need for, because when it was written there was nothing to be platform-limited about |
| ~~P-20~~ | **ADOPTED 2026-09-16 by D-371 and written into Section 8.5.** The amendment to TU-10, as drafted for approval. It reads today: *"Chunk invariance: the PRNG stream, the stimulus draws and the kernel give the same result whole or in chunks of K, and a driver that re-seeds or restarts the step index per chunk is caught."* Proposed replacement: *"Chunk invariance, in two halves. **Oracle** (`tests/test_chunk.py`): the PRNG stream, the stimulus draws and the reference kernel give the same result whole or in chunks of K, and a driver that re-seeds or restarts the step index per chunk is caught. **Engine** (`tools/cmpchk.py`): all nineteen Section 8.4 requests give byte-identical GOLD and GOUT lines at every swept K, which is where FR-SIM-10 requires the property to be shown."* | The catalogue entry names one test; there are now two, and they prove different things — the oracle half can afford a negative case per threat on a fixture, while the engine half is the one that runs the requests ACC-5's determinism claim rests on. A catalogue that named only the first would leave a reader unable to find the evidence FR-SIM-10 actually asks for |
| ~~P-21~~ | **APPROVED 2026-09-16 by D-377 as the plan of record for Phase G Stages 2, 3 and 4** — `docs/plan/2026-09-16-phase-g-stages-2-3-4.md`, the refinement of P-17's Stages 2 to 4 that D-366 left to be approved individually when reached. Its three open design questions were put to the owner with it and answered as D-378 (K by PARM keyword), D-379 (the stream to its own DD) and D-380 (both guards in `make test`) | P-17 fixed the shape of the live view but not the mechanics, and three of the mechanics were decisions rather than details: where K comes from when the request record has no field for it, which channel the stream shares, and what the regression suite pins. Drafting them as one plan let the costs be stated together — in particular that the literal reading of D-140 would move the 412-byte record and stale every recorded MVS artefact |

## Appendix C. Normative simulation kernel (draft)

> **Status: NORMATIVE** (D-71). The step ordering below was reconciled against Shiu et al.'s published `model.py` in the 2026-09-11 session; TBC-01 is closed. Amendments D-67, D-68 and D-69 are marked inline. ⊕ and ⊗ are correctly rounded binary64 operations evaluated strictly left to right.
>
> **Amended 2026-09-13 by D-190 and D-191** with step 0 and one line in ARRIVALS: the per-neuron compensating input that makes a truncated network stand in for the full brain. This is the first change to Appendix C since it became normative, and it was authorised by the owner on the evidence of VL-66…VL-70 — eleven subcircuit constructions, none of which comes within 62% of the full brain's readout response, because a network small enough for the MVS region loses not merely synaptic mass but the *drivers* of the majority of its own neurons. A network that drops nothing carries `nbias = 0` and runs exactly the version 1.0 arithmetic.

**Coefficients (computed on x86, shipped as bit patterns).** With u = v − V_rest, du/dt = (g − u)/τ_mbr and dg/dt = −g/τ_syn, one step of length dt is u′ = P11·u + P12·g and g′ = P22·g, where:

| Method | P11 | P12 | P22 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Exact propagator (τ_syn ≠ τ_mbr) | e^(−dt/τ_mbr) | τ_syn / (τ_syn − τ_mbr) · (e^(−dt/τ_syn) − e^(−dt/τ_mbr)) | e^(−dt/τ_syn) |
| Forward Euler | 1 − dt/τ_mbr | dt/τ_mbr | 1 − dt/τ_syn |

**State.** For each neuron i: u[i], g[i] (binary64), rfr[i] (int32 refractory steps remaining), spikes[i] and first[i] (int32). A ring buffer ring[0..D−1][0..N−1] (binary64) holds delayed synaptic input. All binary64 state starts at +0.0; rfr starts at 0; first starts at −1. The PRNG is seeded from the request (NR-13).

**Before the first step (amendment D-190, D-191):**

```
0. SELECT THE COMPENSATING INPUT
   if nbias = 0:
       bias = none
   else:
       r = the index minimising |brate[r] - rate|, and the SMALLEST such
           index when two are equally near, so a tie goes to the lower
           rate; a rate beyond either end of brate therefore selects that
           end
       bias = the row of N binary64 values at index r
```

The selection is an integer comparison and is made once per request, never inside the step loop. Interpolating between rows would need an integer-to-binary64 conversion, which the `onf_fp` API of NR-07 does not provide. `brate[0]` is 0 and its row is zero in every neuron (IR-NET-09), so a rate 0 request adds nothing and ACC-2's exact silence holds by construction rather than by measurement.

**For each step t = 0, 1, …, T−1:**

```
1. ARRIVALS
   slot = t mod D
   for i = 0 .. N-1 (ascending):
       g[i] = g[i] (+) ring[slot][i]
       ring[slot][i] = +0.0
       # D-190: exactly one further (+) per neuron per step, and only when
       # the network carries a table.  A network with nbias = 0 must
       # perform the arithmetic of format version 1.0 unchanged, so this
       # is a separate loop and not an unconditional (+) of +0.0 -- under
       # NR-07 an added operation is an added operation even when its
       # operand is zero.
       if bias is not none:
           g[i] = g[i] (+) bias[i]

2. STIMULUS DRAWS
   for each stimulus neuron s (ascending index):
       draw per NR-12 (exactly one accepted draw per neuron per step,
       whether or not s is refractory)
       force[s] = (draw produced a spike)

3. INTEGRATE, DETECT, EMIT
   for i = 0 .. N-1 (ascending):
       if rfr[i] > 0:
           rfr[i] = rfr[i] - 1
           # D-67: g is FROZEN while refractory, matching Shiu's
           # "(unless refractory)" on dg/dt.  u is held at U_reset.
           was_refractory = true
       else:
           a      = P11 (x) u[i]
           b      = P12 (x) g[i]
           u[i]   = a (+) b
           g[i]   = P22 (x) g[i]
           was_refractory = false
       if |g[i]| < G_EPS: g[i] = +0.0         # NR-08
       # D-69: the threshold is STRICT, matching Shiu's eq_th 'v > v_th'.
       if not was_refractory and (u[i] > U_th or force[i]):
           u[i]   = U_reset
           g[i]   = +0.0            # D-67: g is reset on every spike,
                                    # matching Shiu's eq_rst 'g = 0*mV'
           # D-68: stimulus neurons have NO refractory period, matching
           # Shiu's 'rfc = 0' for Poisson targets.
           rfr[i] = 0 if i is a stimulus neuron else REFRACTORY_STEPS
           spikes[i] = spikes[i] + 1
           if first[i] < 0: first[i] = (t + 1) * dt_us
           for each target k of i (ascending, CSR order):
               ring[(t + D) mod D][k] = ring[(t + D) mod D][k] (+) w(i,k)
       force[i] = false
```

Because slot (t + D) mod D equals the slot consumed at the start of step t, spikes emitted at step t arrive at the start of step t + D. D must be at least 1.

**After the last step:** fill the response from the readout neurons (spikes, first), set the step count, and compute the fingerprint per IR-COM-05.

## Appendix D. Verification limits register

| ID | Limit |
|---|---|
| VL-01 | QEMU emulates s390x floating point in software. A native backend admitted under QEMU is not yet proven on real s390x hardware; that proof arrives only in Phase F. |
| VL-02 | Enterprise COBOL compatibility of ONFLYDRV is checked only by a GnuCOBOL IBM-dialect proxy until Phase F. A proxy pass is not proof. |
| VL-03 | If JCC cannot compile SoftFloat 3e, the JCC cross-check covers only the components JCC can build; the Gate G1 record states exactly which. |
| VL-04 | Hercules performance says nothing about real IBM Z performance. All timing figures are lab figures. |
| VL-05 | Fingerprint agreement proves cross-platform consistency, not scientific correctness. Correctness rests on ACC-1 to ACC-4. |
| VL-06 | ACC-4 compares a male connectome with a model calibrated on a female connectome. Agreement shows consistency, not identity. |
| VL-07 | Parameters marked TBC are unverified until Phase C. |
| VL-08 | CRC-32 detects accidental corruption, not deliberate tampering. SHA-256 digests are kept in the x86 manifest only. |
| VL-09 | The performance figure in Section 7 is an arithmetic estimate, not a measurement. |
| VL-10 | No CICS behavior is verified in the MVP. Section 3.7 records design intent only. |
| VL-13 | The network scoped under D-56 contains **40.2% of the MaleCNS connectome's synapses** (125,365,933 of 311,833,243). The remaining 59.8% involve at least one body absent from the body-annotations file — fragments, orphans, or bodies below its minconf-0.5 confidence cut — and cannot be simulated because they have no cell type and no predicted transmitter. Applying D-54 and D-55 on top leaves 39.0%. Any ACC-3 truncation-fidelity result is therefore measured against a full-brain reference that is itself already this restricted, and the figure must not be read as a whole-connectome simulation. |
| VL-12 | The sugar stimulus set approved in D-52 is **pharyngeal and taste-peg** (PhG9, dorsal_tpGRN), selected from MaleCNS connectivity onto the Yao & Scott 2022 Sugar SEL neurons. Shiu et al. stimulate **labellar** sugar GRNs on FlyWire. The two protocols therefore excite different sensory populations, so ACC-4's comparison against the Shiu reference carries this difference on top of the male/female difference already noted in VL-06. The evidence is also thinner than the ranking suggests: gustatory input is only 8.3% of the marker neurons' total input, and PhG9 sends only 2.64% of its output to them. |
| VL-11 | TT-02's reference is built against the 8086 SoftFloat specialization, not the ARM-VFPv2-defaultNaN one the engine uses (D-46), because the latter cannot build a complete library. The two share their arithmetic cores verbatim and differ only in NaN handling, so cases whose operands or expected result are NaN are excluded from TT-02. TT-02 therefore proves nothing about NaN behaviour; FR-SIM-08 aborts on NaN instead. |
| VL-14 | TT-01 is proven on x86 only. On MVS the engine's startup self-test (D-79) references `__udivdi3` and `__umoddi3`, GCC's synthesised 64-bit division helpers, which no other engine object needs; whether GCCMVS and PDPCLIB supply them is unverified until Gate G1 (D-84). A TT-01 pass on x86 therefore says nothing about whether ONFLYENG will even link on MVS. **Answered on 2026-09-11 by VL-21: they are not supplied.** |
| VL-15 | **Gate G1 measurement, 2026-09-11, on TK5 MVS 3.8j under Hercules 4.9.1: GCCMVS cannot build ONFLY's 64-bit integer code.** A twelve-line source with no includes, containing only `v = (u64)0xFFFFFFFFU; v = v + (u64)1U;`, ends in `unable to generate reloads` and `Internal compiler error in ?, at 3590`. Per-operation probes, one job each: **add ICEs**; **multiply and divide compile but Assembler XF rejects the generated code (IFOX00 RC 8)**; subtract, both shifts, bitwise and, and assignment compile, link and run. JCC compiled and ran the identical construct correctly, returning 2^32 as hi=00000001 lo=00000000. Assumption **A-05 is disproved** and risk **R-01 has materialised**; this is not a wrong answer but a failure to build. |
| VL-16 | **The GCCMVS 64-bit addition defect is universal in form, and the engine's chosen mitigation survives it.** Four shapes of 64-bit addition were each compiled in their own job on TK5 on 2026-09-11 — `a + b`, `r += b`, `a + (u64)1U`, and the same split across two statements — and **all four ICE**. The 32-bit-halves form with a hand-propagated carry, which is how SoftFloat's not-FAST_INT64 build adds, **compiled, linked, ran and was correct**: 0xFFFFFFFF + 1 returned hi=00000001 lo=00000000. NR-02 and D-06 chose that build precisely because S/370 has no 64-bit integers, and this measurement shows the choice is load-bearing rather than merely prudent. It does **not** show that SoftFloat 3e itself builds under GCCMVS: that is Spike S1 and has not been attempted. It does show that TT-01 as written cannot be built by GCCMVS at all, since `onfi2p32` adds — the test meant to detect bad 64-bit code generation cannot be compiled by the compiler it was written to test. |
| VL-17 | **The GCCMVS optimisation level is decisive, and ONFLY is pinned to `-O1` (D-100).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11, one job per level, on the identical twelve-line 64-bit addition of VL-15: `-O0` ICEs in the reload pass (`unable to generate reloads`, at 3590); `-O2`, `-O3` and `-Os` ICE at a different place (at 447); **`-O1` alone compiles, assembles, links, runs and returns 2^32 correctly**. Every measurement in VL-15 and VL-16 was taken with no `-O` flag, that is at the default level, and is therefore a statement about `-O0` rather than about GCCMVS as such. `-O1` is not chosen for code quality: it is the only level that threads between two distinct compiler defects, and any future GCCMVS or flag change invalidates every MVS result taken under it. |
| VL-18 | **ONFLY C source cannot reach GCCMVS through the card reader on Hercules' `default` codepage.** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11: ASCII `\|` is delivered as EBCDIC 0x6A and GCCMVS rejects it outright (`stray '\152'`, octal for 0x6A); it is the only character of ``\| ! ^ [ ] { } ~ # @ $ \ ` `` that it will not lex, and ASCII `!` arrives at its normal 0x5A so it is no substitute. `819/037` fixed `\|` and broke three others — `^`, `[` and `]` moved to 0xB0, 0xBA and 0xBB and were rejected — which rules CP037 out, since array subscripts appear throughout the engine. Under **`819/1047`** all thirteen characters lex (D-101). The two failed pages together pin GCCMVS's source character set to IBM-1047 by measurement rather than assumption. **Every MVS compile result in this register is conditional on the codepage in force**, which is host configuration and is not carried in the repository: a lab restart returns Hercules to `default` and silently reintroduces this fault. |
| VL-19 | **GCCMVS at `-O1`, 64-bit operations, measured one job each on TK5 on 2026-09-11** with a = 0x0123456789ABCDEF, b = 0x00000000FEDCBA98, every result compared against the value computed on x86 and the operand itself printed back and checked inside each job. **Correct:** MUL (64×64), SHL by a *constant* count, SHR, SUB, AND, OR, and the 32×32→64 widening multiply that `s_mul64To128M.c` relies on. **DIV and MOD** compile but Assembler XF rejects the generated code (IFOX00 RC 8), as at the default level. **ADD** ICEs at 6970 in a realistic shape even though VL-17's minimal shape passes, so the addition defect is context-dependent rather than cured. **The one silent wrong answer is the variable-count left shift:** sweeping `a << n` for n = 0…63 with n a variable, **32 of the 64 counts are wrong**, and they are exactly the 32 for which the result's bit 63 should be set — `a` has exactly 32 bits set. **GCCMVS's variable 64-bit left shift always writes zero into bit 63.** The same counts as compile-time constants — including 1, 9, 10 and 11, the counts SoftFloat 3e actually uses — are all correct. **Withdrawn:** the first version of this entry reported MUL returning zero and `a << 7` dropping bit 63. Both were artifacts of the probe, not of the compiler: it built its operand with `(a << 32) + lo`, and 64-bit `+` is itself defective here, so the scaffolding shared a defect with its subject. With the operand built by OR and verified in-job, both operations are correct. The lesson is recorded rather than quietly fixed, because a probe that can produce a false positive of exactly the kind it is hunting is the same failure class as the blind golden fixture of D-70. |
| VL-20 | **SoftFloat 3e, as ONFLY builds it, depends on the one GCCMVS operation that is silently wrong.** VL-19 measured that a variable-count 64-bit left shift always writes zero into bit 63, while constant counts are correct. Every variable-count 64-bit left shift in ONFLY's actual build was then enumerated: `s_normSubnormalF64Sig.c:48` (`sig<<shiftDist`), `s_normRoundPackToF64.c:51` and `:54` (`sig<<(shiftDist - 10)` and `sig<<shiftDist`), `s_shiftRightJam64.c:46` (`a<<(-dist & 63)`, whose value feeds the sticky bit), `s_shortShiftRightJam64.c:45` (`((uint_fast64_t) 1<<dist) - 1`, where losing bit 63 at dist = 63 turns the mask from 0x7FFF…F into 0xFFFF…F) and ONFLY's own `softfloat/onfsub.c:120` (`sigDiff<<shiftDist`). These are the normalisation, rounding and sticky-bit paths — the places where a dropped top bit changes a result rather than merely a flag. The prediction is therefore that a SoftFloat 3e built by GCCMVS would compute wrong binary64 results **with no diagnostic at any stage**, which is the worst available outcome for a project whose premise is bit-exact reproducibility. **This was analysis, not measurement:** the shift defect was measured in isolation and the call sites were read from the source. **Superseded on 2026-09-11 by VL-21, which measured it and found the prediction wrong in the direction that matters: SoftFloat 3e does not reach the point of computing anything.** Neither affected function can be built — `s_shiftRightJam64.c` compiles and Assembler XF rejects the output, `s_shortShiftRightJam64.c` ICEs — so the failure is loud rather than silent. The shift-site enumeration above stands and remains the reason those sites matter if a compiler is ever found that builds them. NR-03 and D-06 anticipate exactly this case, SoftFloat 2c implementing binary64 in 32-bit integers only. |
| VL-21 | **Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11 (D-104): SoftFloat 3e cannot be built by GCCMVS, and the reason is a missing runtime, not a wrong answer.** ONFLY's NR-04 shims were written and the vendored sources submitted unmodified. `tests/tstsfs.c` compiled and assembled clean (RC 0), so **the shims work on MVS** and `<stdint.h>` resolves from the VB/255 library. Of the two SoftFloat units: `s_shortShiftRightJam64.c` **ICEs at 6970**, and `s_shiftRightJam64.c` **compiles** and is then rejected by Assembler XF with `IFO188 @@UCMPDI IS AN UNDEFINED SYMBOL`. Two separate defects sit behind that one message. First, **PDPCLIB supplies none of the 64-bit helpers GCCMVS calls**: a minimal assembly declaring `@@UCMPDI` (unsigned 64-bit compare, `__ucmpdi2`), `@@UDIVDI` (`__udivdi3`) and `@@UMODDI` (`__umoddi3`) EXTRN and linked against PDPCLIB.NCALIB left **all three unresolved** (IEW0132). This answers VL-14 and D-84 definitively: they are not supplied. Second, **GCCMVS emits `L 15,=A(@@UCMPDI)`**, an A-type address constant, where an external routine needs `=V(...)` or an EXTRN — so the failure surfaces at assembly time as an undefined symbol instead of cleanly at link time as an unresolved reference. The consequence is decisive for the gate: `a != 0` on a `uint64_t` compiles into a call to `@@UCMPDI`, that expression is pervasive in SoftFloat, and there is nothing to link it to. The DIV and MOD rejections in VL-19 are the same defect with `@@UDIVDI` and `@@UMODDI`. |
| VL-22 | **SoftFloat 2c's `bits32` build agrees with TestFloat on all six operations ONFLY uses (D-106).** Measured on x86-64 Windows 11, MinGW gcc, `make c2c`: **260,376 cases checked, 0 mismatches** — add 43,396, sub 43,396, mul 43,390, lt 43,398, le 43,398, eq 43,398, with 18,408 NaN cases excluded per VL-11. `make tt02` runs Release 3e through the **same** generator and vectors and also passes, so the two libraries agree with the same independent oracle. That is the basis for expecting ACC-5 fingerprints to survive the NR-03 fallback. **What this does not prove.** It is agreement on TestFloat's level-1 vector set, not a proof of bit-identity on every input: TestFloat samples the interesting cases (boundaries, subnormals, rounding ties, cancellations) rather than enumerating 2^128 operand pairs, so this is very strong evidence and not a theorem. **No golden-suite fingerprint has been recomputed with 2c** — that needs an `onf_fp` backend, which does not exist, and 2c's `float64` is a two-word struct rather than a scalar so the backend is real work. **Nothing here was measured on MVS:** 2c has never been compiled by GCCMVS, so the reason it was chosen is still unverified on the platform it was chosen for. Two configuration facts are recorded because they are easy to mistake for defects: 2c's shipped `float_detect_tininess = float_tininess_after_rounding` was **kept**, since it gates only `float_raise(float_flag_underflow)` and never the returned value, and ONFLY discards flags (D-34, NR-10); and `float_raise` itself is generated as a no-op, which is D-34 applied to 2c exactly as it was to 3e. |
| VL-23 | **GCCMVS resolves an `#include "name"` to the PDS member given by the first eight characters of the name, uppercased — extension and punctuation included — and that makes SoftFloat 2c untransportable through a PDS as it stands.** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11 with a single member `SOFTFLOA` on the INCLUDE DD and one compile per spelling: `"softfloat.h"`, `"softfloat-macros"`, `"softfloat-specialize"` and `"softfloa.h"` **all four resolved to it**, RC 0. 2c's `bits32/softfloat.c` contains `#include "milieu.h"`, `"softfloat.h"`, `"softfloat-macros"` and `"softfloat-specialize"`; the last three therefore name **one** member, and three different files cannot occupy it. Concatenating several libraries on the DD does not help, since MVS resolves a member from the first library that has it, so every one of the three would still read the same file. The name cannot be changed without editing `third_party/`, which D-28 and D-35 forbid. This is a property of the compiler's include resolution, not of the transport: the same limit applies to any PDS-based build, and it is why Release 3e's flat `s_*.c` names never ran into it. |
| VL-24 | **D-109's probe: GCCMVS offers no include mechanism that escapes VL-23's eight-character member rule.** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11. The compiler's own option list was read first (`PARM='--help'` and `PARM='-v --help'`), which is the full space rather than a guess: the preprocessor does accept `-I`, `-I-`, `-isystem`, `-idirafter`, `-iprefix`, `-iwithprefix`, `-include`, `-imacros`, `-nostdinc` and **`-remap`**. `-remap` is GCC's mechanism for exactly this situation — "Remap file names when including files", intended for filesystems permitting only very short names — and it reads a `header.gcc` map in each include directory. It was tested with a map `softfloat-macros sfmacros`, carried under all three plausible member spellings for `header.gcc` (HEADER, HEADERG, HEADERGC) alongside distinct members SFMACROS (42) and SOFTFLOA (7), with the program returning the macro so the GO step's condition code is the readout. **It compiled clean, no unrecognised option, and returned 0007** — the map was never applied. That is consistent with the port having no directory for such a map to live in: an include resolves to `DD(MEMBER)` directly, so there is nothing for `-remap` to read. The path-based options (`-I` and its relatives) change *which* library is searched, never how a name becomes a member, so none of them can help either. `-include` and `-imacros` force a file in ahead of the source but do not stop `softfloat.c`'s own nested includes from resolving to the wrong member. |
| VL-25 | **SoftFloat 2c compiles under GCCMVS; Assembler XF then rejects it because its external names collide at eight characters — C-04, biting for real.** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11 (D-108, D-110). With the amalgamation in place and `-pedantic-errors` dropped for the vendored unit only — upstream warns in `float64_rem` about pointer signedness and `third_party` is not edited (D-28, D-35) — **COMP1 returned RC 0: 2c's `bits32` build compiles on MVS 3.8j.** ASM1 then returned RC 8 with, repeatedly, `IFO196 FLOAT64@ HAS BEEN PREVIOUSLY DEFINED`, `IFO196 FLOAT32@ HAS BEEN PREVIOUSLY DEFINED`, `IFO196 INT32@TO HAS BEEN PREVIOUSLY DEFINED` and `IFO189 INVALID ENTRY OPERAND, LINKAGE CANNOT BE PERFORMED`. That is upstream's documented extension point: defining the name as a macro means "the platform supplies this", and upstream then omits **both** its declaration and its definition. So a `#define` that merely renames one of those five does not rename it, it **deletes** it — a silent removal of a primitive, which is the failure class this project keeps closing. `__udivdi3` and `__umoddi3` also appear over eight characters but are compiler-supplied helpers reserved to the implementation, not names ONFLY chooses, and VL-21 already records that PDPCLIB does not have them. |

| VL-34 | **D-129's verification, from Raincode's own documentation on 2026-09-11. It corrects an assertion of mine and leaves one question open that only the owner can close.** **Corrected:** I had said `EXEC CICS` could not be compiled on anything available here. It can. The Raincode COBOL compiler "supports embedded SQL and CICS statements natively, **without requiring a precompilation step**", there is a free edition with **no time limit** on Windows or Linux via .NET 6, and their Stack guide states "Native support for a basic CICS subset, **implemented directly at the compiler level**". The compiler turns those statements into calls into a runtime: "these statements translate to calls to the legacy program execution context". **The runtime is QIX**, Raincode's CICS emulator, and its documentation covers what ONFLY's Section 3.7 actually needs — `LINK` with `PROGRAM(name)` and `COMMAREA(data-area)`, conversion of "CICS SEND MAP to 3270 output datastream", and a Terminal Server handling **TN3270 connections** including "the primary mapping support component (BMS)". **Still open, and it is the load-bearing fact:** Raincode's documentation nowhere states whether QIX is included in the free compiler download or is separately licensed, so "compiles `EXEC CICS`" and "runs CICS transactions" are not yet known to be the same offer. The download requires registration, which is the owner's to perform, not mine. **A side finding worth keeping:** QIX speaks TN3270, and so does TK5's VTAM, so a single free 3270 client would serve both and TBD-17 is not a Raincode-specific obstacle. **Not proven:** nothing was installed, compiled or run; every statement here is Raincode's own claim about its product, not a measurement, and the "basic subset" qualifier is theirs. |
