| VL-37 | **The free Raincode edition runs the program half of CICS and refuses the terminal half: probe 1 passes, probes 2 and 3 compile and cannot execute (D-129).** Measured on this Windows host on 2026-09-11 against Raincode Crossbow net8.0 in `C:\Program Files\Raincode\Crossbow\net8.0\bin`, with **no `.rclic` licence file anywhere in the installation**. **What runs.** `cobrc.exe` compiled all four probe programs clean, exit 0, in the MVT-intersection dialect C-03 fixes, with `:FatalMissingIncludes=TRUE` set so `COPY ONFCOM` is known to have resolved against the real `generated/ONFCOM.cpy` rather than silently vanishing. `rcbms.exe` assembled `ONFCMS.bms` into a physical map and a symbolic map, and with `-GenBasedSymbolicMap=true` produced the IBM DSECT shape, `01 ONFCMPO REDEFINES ONFCMPI`, which `ONFCMAP.cbl` then compiled against unchanged. `rclrun.exe -Qix=true ONFCLNK` **ran probe 1 and it passed**: `EXEC CICS LINK` dispatched `ONFCENG`, and the 412-byte COMMAREA came back intact — `ONF-RC` 7, `ONF-OUT-COUNT` 2, `ONF-STEPS` 10000 and the `S9(4)` and `S9(9) COMP` output slots all as written, with the request fields unchanged. A separate scratch capability matrix, every statement carrying `RESP` so that a condition reports itself instead of abending, found `WRITEQ TS`, `READQ TS` (an exact 24-byte round trip), `ASKTIME`, `FORMATTIME` and `ASSIGN` all returning `RESP` 0. **What does not run.** `SEND TEXT`, `SEND MAP` and `RECEIVE` each fail with "not implemented" — `INVREQ`, `RESP` 16, `RESP2` 999 — under `rclrun`, which holds no terminal facility. Terminal statements belong to the Terminal Server, and **`QIX.TerminalServerRunner.exe` and `QIX.Cmd.exe` both refuse to start**, with "No valid license found. You are not allowed to use this program any more" followed by "Scanned folder for (\*.rclic)". The Installation Guide documents only how to *relocate* an existing licence file during an upgrade and never how to obtain one. The terminal server further wants `-ConfigConnectionString`, described as "the SQL Server connection string to the database where the region configuration will be fetched", so even holding a licence there is a region database to stand up before any screen appears. **This corrects VL-36.** That entry read an installer's File table and concluded that "compiles `EXEC CICS`" and "runs CICS transactions" are the same offer. They are the same offer for program-to-program CICS and they are not for terminal CICS: the binaries do ship, and the two that drive a 3270 will not start without a licence file the free download does not include. **An incidental hazard worth keeping.** `cobrc` silently truncates source past column 72 with no diagnostic — a `VALUE` literal beginning in column 55 was cut to its first 17 characters and compiled clean, and the loss showed up only as short data on a queue readback, costing two runs before it was found. D-93 already holds ONFLY's source to 80 columns; this says the 72-column boundary needs mechanical checking too, because this compiler will not report crossing it. **What this does not prove.** Nothing about the mainframe: TK5 still cannot compile `EXEC CICS` at all, which VL-32 settles separately. Nothing about fidelity, since QIX is an emulation of CICS and VL-02's caveat that a proxy pass is not proof applies here one level up. Nothing about whether Raincode would issue a licence on request, which is a commercial question that no measurement on this host can settle. And nothing about the engine boundary: these probes LINK COBOL to COBOL, while ONFLY's engine is C89 and how a Raincode COBOL program on .NET would reach it remains unasked. |
| VL-38 | **INTERCOMM's licence text, obtained and read on 2026-09-11, and what it does and does not settle for TBC-18.** The terms ship in the front matter of the INTERCOMM manuals, not with the TK5 package — `mvs-tk5/Packages/intercomm/` holds only a job log and a DASD volume, and no licence file exists anywhere in the lab directory. Read from the Release 11 DSECTS manual (September 1998) at `intercomm.pocnet.net`, header "LICENSE: INTERCOMM TELEPROCESSING MONITOR". **The terms.** Copyright is held by **Tetragon LLC**, 2005 and 2022 — the SRS previously said "Tetragon Software LLC", which is **corrected here**. It is three-clause-BSD in shape: redistribution and use in source and binary form, with or without modification, permitted on three conditions. Clause 2 requires source redistributions to retain the copyright notice, the conditions and the disclaimer; clause 3 requires binary redistributions to reproduce them in the accompanying documentation or materials; and the standard BSD warranty disclaimer follows. Clause 1 is the one that matters and it reads, in full, "Use or redistribution in any form, including derivitave works, must be for non-commercial purposes only" — the misspelling is in the original. **Why this collides with D-62.** The repository is MIT, and MIT grants every recipient the right to use the work commercially. INTERCOMM forbids that for derivative works. The two cannot both govern one file: ONFLY cannot promise onward commercial rights over anything that is a derivative work of INTERCOMM. **What is not in question.** The owner's own use is non-commercial, so running INTERCOMM in the lab and building a demonstrator on it breaches nothing. The collision is entirely about what the **repository** promises to third parties, not about whether ONFLY may use INTERCOMM. **What the Release 11 DSECTS list shows about the exposure.** An INTERCOMM COBOL subsystem draws on named macros and copybooks — `MSGHDR` (the message header), `COBDSECT` (the COBOL internal prefix area), `PVRBTBLE` (the BTVERB verb table area), `SPALIST` (the system parameter area). These are the things TBC-18 is about, and a subsystem cannot avoid the message header. **What this does not settle, and cannot be settled by measurement.** Whether a given file counts as a derivative work is a legal question, not an engineering one, and nothing here is legal advice. The clause is also unusually broad — "use ... in any form" is wider than the redistribution-focused language of the BSD licence it otherwise copies — and I found no Tetragon interpretation, FAQ or precedent addressing it. What *is* an engineering question, and is put to the owner, is whether any INTERCOMM-derived material needs to enter the MIT repository at all: D-130 already places ONFLY's real transaction source in `EXEC CICS`, which leaves the INTERCOMM side as demonstration scaffolding rather than product. |
| VL-39 | **A 3270 client exists on the development host and reaches TK5's VTAM, measured rather than asserted (TBD-17, D-127, D-131).** The owner installed wc3270 4.5ga6 on 2026-09-11 from `x3270.miraheze.org`, into `C:\Program Files\wc3270`. What closes TBD-17 is not that the package is present but what it does: `python tools/tn3270.py` connects to the running TK5 on `127.0.0.1:3270`, is answered by Hercules on device `0:00C0` — one of the local 3270 devices `tk5.cnf` assigns to VTAM at `00C0` through `00C6` — presses Enter, and lands on VTAM's screen showing `Terminal CUU0C0`, `MVS 3.8j Level 8505 - Update 5`, `RUNNING TK5` and the prompt `Logon ===>`. **Why the logon prompt is the thing that matters.** D-131 puts Phase G's 3270 behind INTERCOMM, and INTERCOMM runs as a VTAM APPL that a terminal logs on to. Reaching Hercules' device banner would only have shown that a socket opened; reaching VTAM's prompt shows the path that Phase G actually needs is open end to end. **A finding worth more than the closure.** The package ships `ws3270.exe`, a scriptable build, so a 3270 session can be driven and its screen captured with no human watching a window. That is what let TBD-17 close on a measurement, and it means Phase G's demonstration can be regression-tested rather than only performed — a 3270 flow that can be asserted against is a different kind of artefact from one that has to be shown. `tools/tn3270.py` carries the recipe, including the part that cost debugging: passing the host on ws3270's command line hangs, connecting but never reading stdin, while issuing `Connect(host:port)` as the first action works at once. **Not proven.** No logon was performed: consuming a TSO session would have changed lab state for no additional evidence. Nothing here shows INTERCOMM accepting a session, because INTERCOMM was not running — VL-32 started and closed it. Nothing here shows a transaction, a BMS map or an ONFLY screen; those are Phase G's work. And the same client is known to speak to Raincode's QIX terminal server (VL-34), but VL-37 established that server will not start without a licence, so that half remains untestable here. |
| VL-40 | **What clock an ONFLY program has on MVS 3.8j, measured before Gate G3 depended on it.** Gate G3 must produce a measured time per neuron-step and NFR-PERF-01 states its bound in wall-clock seconds, so both rest on a clock; D-92 is on record for what guessing at an interface costs, and PDPCLIB's coverage of `<time.h>` is not documented anywhere in this lab. `python tools/mvsclk.py` submitted `tests/tstclk.c` on its own — one small translation unit, no ONFLY headers and no SoftFloat, so the answer came back in a 3.6-second round trip instead of behind a full engine compile. **Three findings.** `clock()` is present and links, but **returns -1 on every call**: PDPCLIB stubs it, even though `CLOCKS_PER_SEC` is defined as 1000. A program that trusted the macro and never checked the return would silently measure nothing. `time()` works and returns a correct epoch value, at **one-second resolution** — the ten-chunk probe loop completed inside a single second and every reading was identical, so it is usable only for intervals of tens of seconds. Third and most useful, **MVS itself reports per-step CPU time**: `IEF374I STEP /name / STOP ... CPU 0MIN nn.nnSEC SRB 0MIN nn.nnSEC`, at centisecond resolution, issued by the system rather than by the C library. The same listing carries `IEF373I` start records. **Why this shapes G3's design.** No single clock is sufficient: `time()` can attribute a measurement to a specific configuration but is coarse, `IEF374I` is precise but reports only a whole job step and cannot say which of several configurations inside it was slow, and neither is wall clock — which is what NFR-PERF-01 is actually written in, and which only the submitting host can see, since it alone includes Hercules' own overhead. `tools/mvsprf.py` therefore reports all three rather than collapsing them, and a disagreement between the program's own arithmetic and MVS's accounting would be worth more than either number alone. **Incidental, and reassuring for the lab rather than for G3:** the whole submit-compile-assemble-link-run-harvest cycle for a single small C file took **3.6 seconds** of host wall clock, and the GCCMVS compile step 0.23 s of CPU. |
| VL-41 | **Gate G3's measurement: one request at the standard duration on MVS 3.8j, and the two configurations that fit inside NFR-PERF-01 (SR-EXT-03, D-37, TBD-06).** Taken on TK5 on 2026-09-11 by `python tools/mvsprf.py`, which builds the SOFT2C engine as VL-33 and VL-35 did and runs `tests/tstprf.c`: one `onfrun` call of 10,000 steps — D-37's provisional 1000 ms standard duration at `dt` 100 us — at each N in SR-EXT-03's sequence. It measures the workload NFR-PERF-01 is written about rather than a micro-benchmark. **The result.** N=250, 102 s per request, 40.8 us per neuron-step. N=500, 198 s, 39.6 us. N=1000, 385 s, 38.5 us. N=2000, 753 s, 37.7 us. N=4000, 1462 s, 36.6 us. Every configuration returned `rc=0`, and each produced 421 spikes, which is bit-for-bit what the same program produces on x86 — so this timed the same computation, not a degraded one. **Against NFR-PERF-01's five-minute bound, only N=250 and N=500 fit.** N=1000 exceeds it by 28%, N=2000 by 151%, N=4000 by 387%. SR-EXT-03's candidate sequence is therefore cut from five values to two on this host, which is a measurement and no longer a fear. **It corrects D-128's estimate, in the unfavourable direction.** That entry estimated "two to three orders of magnitude slower than x86" and said plainly it was not a measurement. The same harness and the same SOFT2C backend on this x86 host gives 90.5 ms at N=250 rising to 1.235 s at N=4000, that is 31 to 36 ns per neuron-step, so MVS is **about 1,180 times slower** — past the top of the estimated range rather than inside it. **What this costs D-128's live view:** at N=250, one second of simulated time takes 102 seconds of wall clock, so a streamed view runs about 100x slower than real time. Watchable, but not live in any naive sense, and TBD-19's interface has to be designed knowing it. **Why the first two attempts were discarded rather than reported.** The first abended SB37 on the object deck: 1.1 MB of static arrays at N=4000 is more than the assembler will punch, so the harness allocates instead — which is also how the CICS path will obtain per-request storage. The second ran while the host entered Modern Standby three times after its lid was closed, and is the reason this entry exists in the form it does. **A trap worth recording, because the obvious guard fails.** `time()` is driven from the host clock through Hercules, so across a host sleep the guest clock advances while almost nothing executes and every interval is silently inflated. The natural cross-check — compare elapsed against the CPU time MVS charges the step — **does not work**: the contaminated job reported `CPU 61MIN 21.92SEC` against 61 minutes 32 seconds elapsed, a ratio of 1.00, while roughly 38 of those minutes were spent asleep. MVS's accounting is derived from a timer Hercules drives from the same host clock, so guest CPU and guest wall inflate together. **Every clock visible inside the guest shares this defect.** Only host CPU charged to the Hercules process can see it, and `tools/mvsprf.py` now certifies on that and refuses to print a G3 result it cannot certify. The run reported here was separately confirmed against the Windows event log: zero Modern Standby transitions between 18:04:36 and 18:53:31, its entire window. **Superseded in one respect by VL-42:** the duration-headroom figures derived here assume cost scales linearly with steps, which VL-42 measured to be false — activity accumulates and per-neuron-step cost rises about 14% between 1000 ms and 1500 ms. Treat them as upper bounds, not predictions. **What it does not establish.** The synthetic network holds activity constant at 421 spikes at every N, because the same eight stimulus neurons drive it, so this is a quiet network; the per-neuron update dominates so heavily that edge traffic is negligible, which is also why the per-neuron-step cost barely moves across a 16x range of N, but a dense and highly active network would cost more. Each figure is a single timing, `reps=1`, though at 102 to 1,462 seconds the one-second quantisation is immaterial. It says nothing about the real MaleCNS network, which Gate G2 must still deliver to MVS. And it is one host: NFR-PERF-01 names the TK5 reference host, and this is that host on this day, with nothing else running. |
| VL-42 | **D-136's 1500 ms maximum is refuted by direct measurement, and the extrapolation that justified it was wrong in the unfavourable direction (TBD-06, D-133, D-136).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11 by `python tools/mvsprf.py --verify`, which compiles `tests/tstprf.c` with `ONFPRF_STEPS` 15000 and `ONFPRF_ONLY` 1000 and runs that one configuration: `PERF n=1000 e=3979 reps=1 secs=658 spikes=624 rc=0`. **658 seconds against D-133's 600-second bound — an overrun of 58 seconds, 9.7%.** D-136 predicted 578 s, so the prediction was low by 80 seconds, 13.8%. Certified from outside the guest: Hercules was charged 678.3 s of host CPU over 684.3 s elapsed, 99% of one core sustained, and the Windows event log independently shows no Modern Standby transition after 23:32:15, the run's entire window. **Why the extrapolation failed, which matters more than that it did.** D-136's 578 s was VL-41's 385 s scaled by 1.5, on my reasoning that scaling in steps is safe "because every step performs identical work". **That reasoning is wrong and is withdrawn.** Steps are not identical: activity accumulates as the network warms up, and the spike count rose from 421 at 10,000 steps to 624 at 15,000 — 48% more spikes for 50% more steps — so later steps traverse more edges than earlier ones. The per-neuron-step cost rose with it, from 38.5 us at 10,000 steps to **43.9 us at 15,000**, up 14.0%, which is almost exactly the size of the prediction error. **A consequence for VL-41 that must not be missed.** VL-41's duration-headroom column was computed by the same linear assumption and is therefore **optimistic at every N**: those figures are upper bounds on what fits, not predictions of it, and the error grows with the size of the extrapolation. The longest duration measured to fit at N=1000 is 1000 ms (385 s, VL-41); 1500 ms is measured not to fit. Dividing this run linearly suggests about 1368 ms, but that is the same class of estimate this entry just refuted and is recorded as an indication only. **A reporting defect found and fixed in the same run.** `tools/mvsprf.py` divided by the default `STDSTEPS` rather than by the step count the deck was compiled with, so the first `--verify` output printed 65.8 us per neuron-step instead of 43.9 — an error of exactly the ratio 15000/10000. It was caught because the figure was implausible against VL-41's 38.5 us. `report()` now takes the step count it is reporting on, and the header line names the duration rather than calling it "the standard duration". **What this does not establish.** It is one configuration, N=1000 at 1500 ms, and one sample. It does not measure where between 1000 ms and 1500 ms the bound is actually crossed, and after this entry no interpolation between them should be trusted to find it — that needs measuring. It says nothing about whether the same super-linearity holds at other N, though the mechanism, accumulating activity, is not specific to N=1000. And it inherits every limit VL-41 records: a quiet synthetic network, one host, one day. |
| VL-43 | **The crossing point at N=1000 is bracketed between 1300 ms and 1500 ms, and the cost curve steepens in a way the spike count does not explain (D-137, TBD-06).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-11 by `python tools/mvsprf.py --verify --steps 13000`: `PERF n=1000 e=3979 reps=1 secs=507 spikes=538 rc=0`. **507 seconds against the 600-second bound — it fits, with 93 seconds of margin, 15.5%.** Certified from outside the guest: 525.6 s of host CPU charged to Hercules over 533.7 s elapsed, 98% of one core, and the Windows event log shows no Modern Standby transition after 23:47, the run's whole window. **The measured bracket at N=1000 is now 1000 ms = 385 s, 1300 ms = 507 s, 1500 ms = 658 s**, so NFR-PERF-01's bound is crossed somewhere between 1300 and 1500 ms. **An observation recorded without an explanation, because I do not have one.** The marginal cost per millisecond of simulated time nearly doubles across the bracket: 0.407 s/ms between 1000 and 1300 ms, then 0.755 s/ms between 1300 and 1500 ms. Spike counts — 421, 538, 624 — grow roughly in proportion to the step count throughout, so the acceleration is not simply more spikes, and the per-neuron-step figures behave the same way: 38.5 us, 39.0 us, then 43.9 us, nearly flat and then jumping. A plausible mechanism would be activity concentrating in high-degree neurons so that each spike traverses more edges than the last, but nothing here measures that and it is offered as a question rather than a finding. What matters operationally does not depend on the cause: **the curve steepens, so extrapolating upward remains unsafe**, which is the third time tonight that has been true and is exactly what D-137 exists to stop. **Not established.** Where between 1300 and 1500 ms the bound is actually crossed — linear interpolation over this bracket suggests about 1423 ms, and that is the class of estimate VL-42 refuted, offered as an indication only. Whether the same steepening occurs at N=250 or N=500, which is unmeasured. And every limit VL-41 records: a quiet synthetic network whose activity is driven by eight stimulus neurons, one host, one day, single samples. |
| VL-44 | **Why the x86 NATIVE backend is slower than the software one, and what that does and does not imply for Phase F. This corrects a caution of mine.** Measured on this x86 host on 2026-09-11 with `tests/tstprf.c`, the same harness as VL-41, one request at the 1000 ms standard duration. **NATIVE is 3.6x to 4.3x slower than SOFT2C**: at N=250, 322.6 ms against 90.5 ms; at N=1000, 1312 ms against 322.6 ms; at N=4000, 5250 ms against 1235 ms. Both produced 421 spikes per repetition, so the same computation was timed. **The cause, read from the source rather than guessed.** `onf_f64` is deliberately `struct { onf_u32 hi, lo; }` so that 64-bit integers cannot leak into engine state, and IR-COM-04 holds it big-endian on every platform. The native backend must therefore convert on **every operation**: `onffadd` is `onf_frdb(onf_todb(a) + onf_todb(b))`, and `onf_todb` disassembles the struct a byte at a time with eight shift-and-mask operations before `memcpy`, while `onf_frdb` copies back and runs a four-iteration byte-swap loop. Three such conversions per arithmetic operation. **SOFT2C pays almost nothing by comparison:** SoftFloat 2c's bits32 build declares `typedef struct { bits32 high, low; } float64;`, structurally identical to `onf_f64`, so `onf_to2c` and `onf_fr2c` are two field assignments each and no byte ever moves. **Two consequences, and the second corrects me.** First, **the MVS measurements in VL-41, VL-42 and VL-43 are not inflated by marshalling**: MVS runs SOFT2C, whose conversion is free, so that 1,180x figure is genuine emulation and software-float cost and nothing else. Second, I had cautioned that a hardware-float backend on z/OS would pay the same marshalling and that Phase F should therefore not be assumed to lift the performance ceiling. **That caution was too pessimistic and the evidence I gave for it does not transfer.** The expensive half of the conversion is the byte-swap loop, and it is guarded by `#ifdef ONF_FP_LITTLE` — on a big-endian host such as z/OS or Linux s390x it is not compiled at all. The residual cost there is the eight shift-and-mask operations, which is real but much smaller. **Not measured, and it is the measurement that would settle it:** no big-endian native build has been timed. Gate G0 provides Linux s390x under QEMU, so the experiment is available and would separate the endian artifact from the genuine conversion cost. Until then the size of Phase F's likely speedup is argued, not known. Nothing here measures z/OS, which this project has never run on. |
| VL-45 | **GCCMVS cannot compile the 64-bit integer self-test at any optimisation level, and ONFLYENG asks it to — a gap between the program and NR-14 as D-118 amended it (Gate G2, D-141, NR-03, NR-14).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-12 by `python tools/mvseng.py`, four submissions differing only in the `-O` level. `softfloat/onfint.c` fails to compile every time, and the failure moves with the level, which is what rules out a single bad construct: at **-O0 and with no flag**, `unable to generate reloads for:` followed by an internal compiler error, in `onfi2p32` at the 64-bit addition `v = v + (onfi_u64)1U`; at **-O2**, an internal compiler error in the same function; at **-O1**, an internal compiler error at the close of `onfirun`, the switch over 64-bit multiply, divide, modulo, shifts, add, subtract and the two comparisons. "Unable to generate reloads" is a register-allocator failure, so this is the same family as VL-21's finding that GCCMVS cannot build SoftFloat 3e's 64-bit code, and it is why NR-03's fallback to Release 2c exists at all. **It should never have been attempted, and that is the actual finding.** NR-14 as amended by D-118 requires the self-test "for each integer width that platform's engine actually uses", and says explicitly that where NR-03's fallback to Release 2c is in force the engine uses no 64-bit integer and the width is 32 bits, TT-01/32. MVS runs 2c (D-105), so the 64-bit self-test is the wrong test there by the requirement's own words. `tools/mvseng.py` linked it because it mirrored the x86`eng` target, which legitimately uses the 64-bit form because x86 runs 3e. **But the gap is not only in the tool.** `engine/src/onflyeng.c` calls `onfitst()` unconditionally at line 271 and prints `ONF901S 64-BIT INTEGER SELF-TEST FAILED`; it was written under D-78 and D-79 before D-118 amended NR-14 and before NR-03's fallback placed 2c on MVS, and it has no 32-bit path. **ONFLYENG therefore cannot be built on MVS as written**, and closing that is a change to the engine's startup, not to the transport. **What this does NOT indict.** The tape mounted on 0480, and seven of the eight translation units — the amalgamated SoftFloat 2c library, the backend, the float compare and report units, the CRC, the decoder and ONFLYENG itself — all compiled RC 0000 with no diagnostic. Nothing here says anything about the transport, because the job never reached LKED or GO: `COND=(4,LT)` stopped it. Gate G2 has not yet been tested at all, in either direction. |
| VL-46 | **ONFLYENG builds and links on MVS 3.8j for the first time (D-142); the tape transport reaches allocation and is not yet proven (Gate G2, D-141, IR-TRN-01).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-12 by `python tools/mvseng.py`. **What is established.** With D-142's width selection in place, all eight translation units — the amalgamated SoftFloat 2c library, the `onffp2` backend, the float compare and report units, the CRC, the decoder, the **32-bit** integer self-test and `onflyeng.c` itself — compiled and assembled **RC 0000**, and **LKED completed RC 0000**, with no compiler or assembler diagnostic of any kind. Before D-142 the same deck died at COMP7 (VL-45). So the engine program itself is now buildable on MVS, which no earlier verification limit records. **What is not established, and it is Gate G2's whole question.** No transfer has completed, in either direction. The GO step never ran. Three JCL forms were tried for the tape DD and each failure is recorded because each was a different mistake: (1) `UNIT=480,DISP=OLD,LABEL=(1,NL)` with **no DSN** — a DD with `DISP=OLD` and no name is an unnamed *temporary*, which cannot already exist, so `IEF453I JOB FAILED - JCL ERROR` with no annotation against the card and no ALLOC line; (2) adding `DSN=ONFNET` but **no volume** — an uncatalogued dataset sends MVS to the catalog and nothing called ONFNET is catalogued, failing identically and indistinguishably; (3) adding `VOL=SER=ONFNET` — the JCL was **accepted** and allocation was reached, but MVS issued `*IEF233A M 480,ONFNET,,ONFENG,GO` and waited, then `IGF991E MOUNT PENDING FOR DEVICE 480`. Re-issuing `devinit` while MVS held the drive allocated left it `INTERVENTION REQUIRED` and the job never proceeded. **The device was never the problem:** `D U,,,480,4` reports 480 as an online 3400 throughout, and `D R,L` reported it as a READY UNIT even while the mount was pending. What is unresolved is the mount handshake for an **unlabelled** tape — MVS has no label to verify a serial against and appears to be waiting for a ready transition that re-mounting did not supply. **Lab state:** the job was cancelled and 0480 returned to an empty drive; `$DA` reports no active jobs and `D R,L` no outstanding requests, so the shared lab is clean. **Nothing here bears on IR-TRN-01, IR-TRN-02 or IR-TRN-04.** No byte has crossed, so the tape is neither shown to be binary-transparent nor shown not to be, and zero of the ten transfers IR-TRN-02 requires have been performed. Gate G2 is open and untested. |
| VL-47 | **Why every tape mount failed, and how far the tape gets once that is fixed (Gate G2, D-141, supersedes VL-46's diagnosis).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-12. **The root cause was not MVS at all.** This repository lives under a path containing a u-umlaut, and Hercules' `devinit` given that path replies `HHC00221I ... type AWS`, then **`HHC00205E`**, then `HHC02245I device initialized` — an error in the middle, a success message after it, and the drive left **empty**. Every mount this session reported as successful had in fact failed, and MVS was asking for a volume that was never there. **My own check made it invisible.** `mount()` looked for the image's basename in the console reply and reported success when it found it; the console echoes the command it was given, so the check passed on every failed mount. A verification that cannot fail is worse than none, because it is believed. Two further properties of the console hid the evidence: it returns only the last **22** messages unless `msgcount` asks for more, which is how a poller watching for the mount request missed it while the job waited; and it answers **HTTP 400** for a long URL, which is why a scratchpad path could not be used. Staging the image to the lab's own `tape/` directory — short, ASCII — makes `devinit` report `format type AWS Format tape file` with no `HHC00205E`. **A mechanism worth keeping.** `HHC00201I ... tape closed` appears in the syslog between the job starting and `IEF233A`: **MVS unloads the drive during allocation**. A volume mounted before submission is therefore gone by the time it is wanted, and the tape must be loaded in answer to the request, as an operator would. **How far it now gets.** With the path fixed, MVS advances from the allocation-time mount (`IEF233A M 480,ONFNET,,ONFENG,GO`) to the **OPEN-time** mount, `IEC501A M 480,ONFNET,NL,6250 BPI,ONFENG,GO`, which names the label type and the density. Loading in answer to that produces `IEC502E K 480,ONFNET,NL,ONFENG,GO` — dismount and keep — and then the request again: **MVS is refusing the volume, not failing to see it**, and the cause of the refusal is not established. Density, block size against the 3420's characteristics, and what MVS reads in the first block of an unlabelled tape are all untested. **Unchanged from VL-46: zero transfers have been performed.** IR-TRN-01, IR-TRN-02 and IR-TRN-04 are untouched, no byte has crossed in either direction, and Gate G2 remains open. The lab was left clean — job cancelled, 0480 empty, no active jobs and no outstanding requests. |
| VL-48 | **BLP is not available on TK5, the tape mount is a two-request handshake, and MVS rejects the volume's content rather than its mounting (D-143, Gate G2).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-12 by `python tools/mvseng.py` with `LABEL=(1,BLP)`. **BLP is silently downgraded.** With `LABEL=(1,BLP)` in the JCL, MVS's own messages report **NL**: `IEC502E K 480,ONFNET,NL,ONFENG,GO` and `IEC501A M 480,ONFNET,NL,6250 BPI,ONFENG,GO`. Bypass label processing is commonly reserved to authorised job classes and TK5's class A is not one, so MVS treated it as NL. D-143 anticipated this outcome as one of two useful ones: **the label hypothesis is therefore untested, not disproven**, and the standard-labelled image becomes the way to test it. **The mount is two requests, not one.** `IEF233A M 480,ONFNET,,ONFENG,GO` is issued at **allocation**; once answered, MVS immediately closes the tape and issues `IEC502E K` followed by `IEC501A M 480,ONFNET,NL,6250 BPI,ONFENG,GO` at **OPEN**. Both must be answered by an operator, which no previous attempt knew. **Answering both does not help.** Each load is followed by `HHC00201I ... tape closed` and another `IEC502E K` — dismount and keep — and the request repeats indefinitely. So MVS reads the volume, rejects what it finds, and asks for a different one: **the refusal is about the tape's content, not about the mounting protocol**, which the two-request discovery might otherwise have been mistaken for. **A defect in my own tooling, found and fixed.** The mount watcher searched the cumulative syslog for `IEF233A` by substring, so on its first poll it matched a **stale request from a previous job** and loaded the tape *before* the current job asked — the one timing that cannot work, because MVS unloads the drive during allocation. The log then read `format type ... / tape closed / IEF233A` in that order, which looks exactly like a mount being ignored. It now ignores every request present before it starts watching. **Still zero transfers.** IR-TRN-01, IR-TRN-02 and IR-TRN-04 remain untouched and Gate G2 remains open. The lab was left clean: no active jobs, no outstanding requests, 0480 empty. |
| VL-49 | **Density is eliminated as the cause of the tape refusal (D-144, Gate G2).** Measured on TK5 on 2026-09-12 by adding `DEN=3` — 1600 BPI — to the `ONFNET` DD's DCB and changing nothing else. **The DCB operand is honoured:** MVS's mount message changed from `IEC501A M 480,ONFNET,NL,6250 BPI,ONFENG,GO` to `IEC501A M 480,ONFNET,NL,**1600 BPI**,ONFENG,GO`, so the density MVS names is the one the DCB asks for and 6250 was merely what it prints when the DCB is silent. **The refusal is unchanged:** the tape loads, MVS closes it, issues `IEC502E K 480,ONFNET,NL,ONFENG,GO` and asks again, exactly as at 6250. Two supporting observations, both read-only. Hercules exposes **no density parameter at all** — `devlist TAPE` reports only `maxsize`, `eotmargin` and `deonirq`, and `help devinit` documents no density argument — so there is nothing on the emulator side for a density to mismatch against. And the network file cannot be mistaken for a label: it begins `4F 4E 46 31`, ASCII `ONF1`, whereas MVS reads labels in EBCDIC where `VOL1` is `E5 D6 D3 F1`. **What remains.** The label hypothesis VL-48 left untested is now the leading candidate and is still untested: that `NL` makes MVS read the first block to confirm it is not a label, and label processing expects an 80-byte record where `tools/mkaws.py` writes 32,720 bytes. Testing it needs either a standard-labelled image or a first block sized to a single record. **Unchanged: zero transfers.** IR-TRN-01, IR-TRN-02 and IR-TRN-04 remain untouched, Gate G2 remains open, and the lab was left clean. |
| VL-50 | **The first network file reaches MVS 3.8j intact, and ONFLYENG verifies it there — the first execution of the engine program on a mainframe (D-141, D-145, IR-TRN-01, IR-TRN-03, FR-LOD-01..05, NFR-OBS-01).** On TK5 on 2026-09-12, `python tools/mvseng.py`: GO step **COND CODE 0000**, and the program printed `ONF001I NETWORK LOADED N=913 E=72852 CRC=9C0A8413`, the NFR-OBS-01 manifest naming `FLOAT BACKEND SOFT2C`, `PLATFORM MVS38J`, `NET FORMAT 1.0`, `HEADER CRC 6E556D23`, `PAYLOAD CRC 9C0A8413`, `NS NR 14 2`, `DT 100 US`, `DT BITS 3FB999999999999A`, `DELAY REFRACT 18 22 STEPS`, `BYTES NEED 885440 1038568`, and finally **`ONF003I VERIFY-ONLY: NETWORK OK`**. **The cross-check that makes it a measurement rather than a claim:** `data/networks/MANIFEST.json` records the path network as 913 neurons and 72,852 edges, and MVS reported exactly those, having derived them from the file's own header after passing all six FR-LOD-02 checks in order — magic constant, byte-order sentinel, format version, header CRC, declared payload length, payload CRC. Raw mode is simply the absence of Hercules' `ascii` and `trunc` options — which are, precisely, the code-page translation and trailing-blank truncation IR-TRN-01 names. **The network file exposed something else.** `python tools/mvseng.py --reader`: the reader delivered the whole file — `devlist` reports `0:010C 3505 * eof IO[11070]` for 11,068 cards, and the hopper is empty — but ONFLYENG ended RC 0012 with **`ONF108E NETWORK DATASET UNREADABLE: DD:ONFNET`**, before any header check ran. **The cause is in ONFLYENG, not the transport.** `onferead()` sizes the dataset by `fseek(f, 0L, SEEK_END)` and `ftell`, and **a card reader is not seekable**, so the read fails at its first statement. `tests/tstxfr.c` succeeded over the same device because it does a plain `fread` into a fixed buffer and never seeks. **This contravenes FR-LOD-03 as written**, which requires ONFLYENG to "reconstruct the network byte stream from fixed-length records and ... trust the header's declared length, **not the dataset size**". Measuring the dataset size is `onferead`'s first action. The requirement anticipated exactly this and the implementation did not follow it; the card reader is simply the first device that made the difference observable, since a seekable dataset hides it. **Status.** IR-TRN-01 is satisfied for the card reader on the test file. The left MN9 ranks 318th; the right MN9 is outside the top 1000 and enters only as a readout. **Subcircuits (SR-EXT-02, `data/networks/SUBCIRCUIT.json`):** n250 256 neurons 3,699 edges; n500 501 and 10,783; n1000 1,001 and 32,929; decoded need 90,472 / 219,580 / 575,332 bytes, all far inside TBD-14's 8M (NFR-MEM-01 PASS) and all inside Gate G3's bound (NFR-PERF-01 PASS by VL-41). **ACC-3 (mean MN9 rate over seeds 1..30, subcircuit versus full brain, tolerance max(10%, 1 Hz)) at 10 / 40 / 60 / 120 / 200 Hz, full brain 3.67 / 14.35 / 28.38 / 68.38 / 88.80:** N = 250: 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 — MN9 silent; N = 500: 0.00 / 8.53 / 18.07 / 29.98 / 37.20 — about half; N = 1000: 0.43 / 32.40 / 50.97 / 81.82 / 101.15 — above the full brain at every rate but 10 Hz. **All fifteen comparisons fail.** **Why:** the left MN9 has 253 presynaptic partners carrying 2,956 excitatory and 2,966 inhibitory synapses, a balanced input; the top-1000-by-activity set keeps 50 of them with 1,177 excitatory against 693 inhibitory synapses (excitation-biased, hence the overshoot), the top 500 keep 20 with 604 against 108, and the top 250 keep 5 with 6 against 14 (silence). | VL-68 | **The D-181 diagnostic brackets the extraction problem: neither the pathway-shaped D-75 fixture nor the entire two-hop neighbourhood reproduces the full brain's MN9 response, so ACC-3's ±10% is not met by any subcircuit construction tried, at any size inside or outside the MVS limits.** Measured 2026-09-13 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc 6.3.0, NATIVE backend, `python prep/extract.py --acc3-file … --label path\|hop2 --jobs 14`, 150 runs each, seeds 1..30, `data/calibration/acc3-path.json` and `acc3-hop2.json`. **Mean MN9 rate at 10 / 40 / 60 / 120 / 200 Hz, full brain 3.67 / 14.35 / 28.38 / 68.38 / 88.80:** `path` (913 neurons, 72,852 edges, 1.04 MB decoded, inside TBD-14): 7.98 / 94.72 / 108.87 / 118.92 / 115.32, four- to sevenfold above the full brain from 40 Hz up; `hop2` (13,521 neurons, 1,704,385 edges, 22.9 MB decoded, outside TBD-14 and Gate G3): 4.78 / 36.25 / 52.35 / 89.55 / 110.38, 25% to 150% above. The sugar-to-MN9 response of this model is a property of the excitation–inhibition balance of the whole network, and no local construction of up to 4,456 neurons reproduces it within ±10%. **Not proven:** two- or more-step closures (all beyond the region); a rule that trims weights rather than neurons (not attempted); anything on MVS, s390x or the SOFT backends |
| VL-70 | **Weight compensation does not rescue truncation either, and the measurement says why: a subcircuit of this size loses not just synaptic mass but the drivers themselves, so most of its own neurons never fire and no gain on a retained edge can make them.** Measured 2026-09-13 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc 6.3.0, NATIVE backend, `python prep/extract.py --compensate --jobs 14`, 8 constructions emitted and 1,200 ACC-3 runs plus 192 rate-matching runs in 609 s; `data/calibration/acc3-compensated.json`. **Mean MN9 rate over seeds 1..30 at 10 / 40 / 60 / 120 / 200 Hz, full brain 3.67 / 14.35 / 28.38 / 68.38 / 88.80:** **A** (D-188 selection only, SR-EXT-02 intact) n250 all zero; n500 0.00 / 5.58 / 10.97 / 16.78 / 20.63; n1000 0.43 / 32.48 / 50.88 / 81.88 / 101.53. **B1** (one-shot mean-field compensation) n250 all zero; n500 1.40 / 17.97 / 23.35 / 34.65 / 47.15; n1000 0.62 / 34.60 / 54.23 / 88.77 / 113.97. **B2** (self-consistent rate matching) n500 13.05 / 17.12 / 19.38 / 28.67 / 51.73; n1000 51.50 / 40.42 / 37.42 / 40.00 / 45.23. **All forty comparisons fail**; the tolerance each would need at its worst rate is 100% (A-n250, A-n500, B1-n250), 126% (A-n1000), 141% (B1-n1000), 62% (B1-n500), 256% (B2-n500) and 1305% (B2-n1000). Decoded need 88 to 562 KB, every one inside TBD-14's 8M. **The mechanism, measured directly.** All 501 and all 1,001 kept neurons fire in the full brain -- SR-EXT-01 selects them for exactly that -- but at 80 Hz, seed 1, only **153 of 501** and **456 of 1,001** fire at all inside their subcircuit. For the other 70% and 54% the neurons that used to drive them are outside the set, so there is no retained edge whose weight a gain could scale. B2's update therefore cannot converge: its median interior relative rate error sits at exactly 1.000 through every iteration (the signature of a neuron that fires in the full brain and is silent in the subcircuit), the ratio clips at 10 each round and the excitatory gain runs to the 20x cap -- median alpha 10.63 at n500 and 3.59 at n1000 -- which amplifies the few neurons that do fire and flattens the rate curve into near-independence of the stimulus (B2-n1000 fires 51.50 Hz at a 10 Hz stimulus and 45.23 Hz at 200 Hz). **Two secondary findings.** A's balance rule is nearly inert: it spends k = 1, 4 and 14 slots because the condition it tests -- inhibition retained at least as well as excitation -- is already met at N = 250 where only 0.1% of the readout's excitatory drive survives, and A-n1000 reproduces SR-EXT-01's top-1000 to within seed noise (0.43 / 32.48 versus 0.43 / 32.40). And the rate-weighted picture corrects VL-66's synapse-count reading: weighting each presynaptic neuron by its full-brain firing, the top-1000 set retains 91% of the left MN9's excitatory drive and 97% of its inhibitory drive, gains 1.1 and 1.0 -- MN9's own input is nearly complete at N = 1000 and the overshoot was never a readout-balance problem. **Reading, with VL-66..VL-69:** eleven constructions have now been measured -- global activity at three sizes, a one-hop partner patch at three sizes, a pathway fixture, a two-hop neighbourhood, an inhibitory closure, a balance-matched selection at three sizes, and two families of weight compensation -- and none is within 62% of the full brain, let alone 10%. The sugar-to-MN9 response of this model is a property of the whole network's excitation-inhibition balance, and it is not recoverable by selecting neurons, by adding their neighbourhoods, or by rescaling what is left. **Not proven:** a construction that changes the model rather than the network -- a compensating bias current, a per-neuron threshold or leak adjustment -- none of which was attempted and each of which would deviate from Appendix C rather than from SR-EXT-02; N above 4,456, which Gate G3 and TBD-14 exclude anyway; anything on MVS, s390x or the SOFT backends; ACC-1 on any of these constructions |
| VL-71 | **The D-190 compensating input does what it was meant to do mechanically — a 250-neuron subcircuit that was silent now responds at every rate — but no size meets ACC-3, and at N = 1000 it makes the fit worse.** Measured 2026-09-13 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc 6.3.0, NATIVE backend. Inputs: `python prep/extract.py --ratebias --jobs 8`, 24 full-brain runs of 1000 ms at the eight TBD-07 rates with seeds 1..3 in 1,403 s, giving `data/calibration/rate-activity.npz`; then `--biasnet --jobs 14`, three networks emitted at format version 1.1 and 450 ACC-3 runs in 217 s, giving `data/calibration/acc3-biasnet.json`. **Mean MN9 rate over seeds 1..30 at 10 / 40 / 60 / 120 / 200 Hz, full brain 3.67 / 14.35 / 28.38 / 68.38 / 88.80:** C-n250 0.00 / 19.00 / 60.03 / 99.50 / 130.50; C-n500 0.05 / 22.23 / 41.42 / 89.97 / 121.07; C-n1000 0.32 / 32.97 / 65.67 / 106.75 / 126.53. Decoded need 108,940 to 647,440 bytes, all inside TBD-14's 8M. **All fifteen comparisons fail.** **Against the uncompensated sets of VL-66, per-rate relative deviation:** N = 250 goes from 100% at every rate — MN9 silent — to 100 / 32 / 112 / 46 / 47%; N = 500 from 100 / 41 / 36 / 56 / 58% to 99 / 55 / 46 / 32 / 36%; N = 1000 from 88 / 126 / 80 / 20 / 14% to 91 / 130 / 131 / 56 / 42%. So the compensation rescues the smallest network from silence and improves the middle one at the top of the range, and **degrades the largest**. **Diagnosis of the degradation, and it is in the input rather than the mechanism.** The per-rate activity the bias is built from is strongly non-monotonic over only three seeds — total network spikes 14,148 / 73,018 / 72,119 / 88,066 / 96,425 / 103,071 / **457,186** / 322,604 at 10 / 20 / 40 / 60 / 80 / 120 / 160 / 200 Hz — because a run either ignites the hyperactive population VL-66 found (191 neurons sustaining 250 to 280 Hz) or does not. A three-seed mean of a bimodal quantity overestimates the typical run, and the effect is visible directly in the tables: the largest single bias value at N = 1000 is **18.55** at the 160 Hz row against 0.16 to 0.59 at every rate up to 120 Hz, and U_th is 7.0 mV. Every compensated network accordingly overshoots at 60 Hz and above. **What the result does establish.** The mechanism is sound where it can be seen in isolation: at N = 250, where VL-66 measured MN9 completely silent because only 5 of its 253 presynaptic partners survive, supplying the dropped drive as a constant input makes the readout fire at every rate. That is the first construction of twelve to change the qualitative behaviour of a truncation rather than only its magnitude. **Not proven:** whether a better estimator of the per-rate activity — more seeds, or a median rather than a mean over a bimodal distribution — brings any size inside ACC-3; nothing was run on MVS, s390x or the SOFT backends; ACC-1 was not evaluated on these networks; and the single-operating-point limitation of D-188 stands, since the table is piecewise constant between sampled rates |
| VL-72 | **D-193's diagnosis is confirmed by the data and its remedy is measured: the 15-seed median removes the overshoot the 3-seed mean caused, and gives the best agreement above the low-rate floor of any construction so far — but no size meets ACC-3, and the 10 Hz comparison turns out to be asking a truncation to reproduce a rare stochastic event.** Measured 2026-09-13 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc 6.3.0, NATIVE backend. `python prep/extract.py --ratebias --jobs 8`: 120 full-brain runs of 1000 ms at the eight TBD-07 rates with seeds 1..15, 6,770 s. `--biasnet --jobs 14`: three networks and 450 ACC-3 runs, 214 s. **The diagnosis, confirmed.** Total network activity, mean over 15 seeds against median over the same 15, at 10 / 20 / 40 / 60 / 80 / 120 / 160 / 200 Hz: 35,126/19,478 (ratio **1.80**), 63,232/61,766 (1.02), 135,283/76,995 (**1.76**), 82,691/83,912 (0.99), 93,460/95,898 (0.97), 102,305/104,846 (0.98), 362,541/254,865 (**1.42**), 156,974/114,678 (**1.37**). The mean stands up to 80% above the median at 10, 40, 160 and 200 Hz and within 3% of it at 20, 60, 80 and 120 Hz — the ignition tail inflates the estimate at exactly the rates where VL-71's networks overshot, and nowhere else. The largest single bias value at N = 1000 falls from 18.55 to 8.12. **ACC-3 with the median bias, mean MN9 rate over seeds 1..30 at 10 / 40 / 60 / 120 / 200 Hz, full brain 3.67 / 14.35 / 28.38 / 68.38 / 88.80:** C-n250 0.00 / 0.00 / 49.50 / 101.00 / 129.00; C-n500 0.28 / 9.55 / 35.52 / 94.20 / 115.25; C-n1000 0.12 / 28.47 / 57.32 / 110.68 / 125.62. **All fifteen comparisons fail.** Relative deviation per rate: C-n500 92 / 33 / 25 / 38 / 30%, against 99 / 55 / 46 / 32 / 36% for the same set on the 3-seed mean (VL-71) and 100 / 41 / 36 / 56 / 58% uncompensated (VL-66). **Two different "best", and they disagree.** On ACC-3's own statistic — the worst rate — the best construction of thirteen is still **B1-n500 at 62%** (VL-70), because it fires 1.40 Hz at 10 Hz where C-n500 fires 0.28. Above that floor, over 40 to 200 Hz, the best is **C-n500 (median) at 38%**, against 49% for B1-n500. Neither is within ACC-3's 10%. **Why the 10 Hz point resists every construction.** VL-64 measured the full brain's own 10 Hz response as 3.67 Hz with a standard deviation of **4.22** over 30 seeds — a spread larger than the mean, because MN9 fires at 10 Hz only in seeds where the network ignites. ACC-3's tolerance there is the 1 Hz absolute floor. So at 10 Hz the criterion asks a 500-neuron truncation to reproduce, to within 1 Hz, the frequency of a rare stochastic event in a 184,099-neuron network. That is a property of the criterion meeting this model, not of any construction. **Not proven:** whether a compensating input that varied within a run, or one fitted per rate rather than measured, would pass — neither was attempted; whether excluding 10 Hz from ACC-3, or raising its floor, is acceptable, which is the owner's to decide; anything on MVS, s390x or the SOFT backends; ACC-1 on any compensated network |
| VL-73 | **The fitted compensating input gives Phase C its best agreement yet — four of ACC-3's five rates inside tolerance at N = 500 in sample, two of five out of sample — and isolates the remaining failure to a single rate whose response no amount of compensation can reach at that size.** Measured 2026-09-13 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc 6.3.0, NATIVE backend, `python prep/extract.py --fitbias --jobs 14`, 3,700 s; `data/calibration/acc3-fitbias.json`. Fit targets are the full brain's own MN9 rate from the 15-seed measurement — 0:0.00 / 10:4.57 / 20:5.63 / 40:15.07 / 60:27.63 / 80:46.20 / 120:67.73 / 160:76.57 / 200:89.63 Hz — whose validation-rate values agree with `acc4.json`'s independent 30-seed run; ACC-3 is judged against `acc4.json` at 30 seeds as VL-66 was. **First, a method finding that invalidated the original search.** MN9's response is **not monotone** in the bias multiplier. Measured at N = 500, 40 Hz, multiplier 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 / 1 / 1.5 / 2 / 3 / 5 / 10 / 20: 8.50 / 12.15 / **15.80** / 11.80 / 9.30 / 6.75 / 6.00 / 2.15 / 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 Hz — it peaks near 0.5 and collapses to silence. At 120 Hz the same sweep rises monotonically, 29.75 to 242.75 Hz. **The cause is the sign of the readout's own compensating value, which changes with rate:** at 40 Hz the left MN9's bias is **−0.45** while the sum over all 501 kept neurons is **+50.68**, so a large multiplier suppresses the readout directly even as it drives everything else harder; at 120 Hz that same value is **+0.45**. A bisection assuming monotonicity returned the 20× cap — the worst multiplier available at that rate — so the search was replaced with the coarse scan plus refinement D-171 chose for the W_syn calibration, for this exact hazard. The full scan is kept in the result file. **Per-rate multipliers at N = 500** (10 seeds, closest to target): 10:1.2492 / 20:0.2570 / 40:0.4000 / 60:0.6032 / 80:0.5519 / 120:0.5781 / 160:0.6455 / 200:0.6235 — flat at about **0.58 from 60 Hz up**, with 20 Hz the outlier. **F1, out of sample** (multiplier `a + b*R`, two parameters fitted on the D-165 calibration rates {20, 80, 160}, then judged on the five validation rates the fit never saw). N = 500, `0.25177 + 0.0026886*R`: 0.00 / 14.00 / 24.57 / 68.03 / 101.38 Hz against 3.67 / 14.35 / 28.38 / 68.38 / 88.80 — **40 Hz PASSES at 2.4% and 120 Hz PASSES at 0.5%**, 60 Hz and 200 Hz miss by 13.4% and 14.2%, 10 Hz fails. Worst deviation above the 10 Hz floor **14%**, against 38% for the best previous construction (VL-72). N = 1000, `0.65062 − 0.0009287*R`: 0.12 / 30.25 / 51.90 / 98.20 / 116.63, every rate fails. **F2, in sample** (one free multiplier per validation row — five parameters against ACC-3's five constraints, so **a pass measures the mechanism's freedom, never truncation fidelity**). N = 500: 0.27 / 14.47 / 28.03 / 67.93 / 89.35 — **four of five rates inside ACC-3**, at 0.8%, 1.2%, 0.7% and 0.6%, the first construction of seventeen to put any rate inside the criterion and much the first to put four. N = 1000: 4.45 / 14.87 / 26.58 / 81.82 / 102.23 — 10, 40 and 60 Hz pass, 120 and 200 fail. **ACC-3 is FAILED by all four**, because it requires all five rates. **The 10 Hz point, now isolated.** At N = 500 the entire scan from multiplier 0 to 20 produced at most **0.10 Hz** of MN9 firing against a 4.57 Hz target: no compensation of any strength makes the 500-neuron subcircuit respond at a 10 Hz stimulus. At N = 1000 it does respond — F2-n1000 reaches 4.45 Hz and **passes** ACC-3 at 10 Hz. So the 10 Hz failure is a function of how much network is kept, not of how the compensation is set, and it is the sole remaining failure at N = 500. Read with VL-72's measurement that the full brain's own 10 Hz response is 3.67 Hz with a standard deviation of 4.22, the criterion at that rate is asking a truncation to reproduce a rare stochastic event. **Not proven:** a one-parameter constant multiplier, which the flat 0.58 cluster above suggests would beat the tilted line out of sample — not attempted; the within-run compensating input of D-194 — not attempted; whether F2's freedom survives at seeds the fit never saw, since F2's 40–200 Hz agreement is in sample by construction; anything on MVS, s390x or the SOFT backends — every number here is x86-64 NATIVE; ACC-1 on any of these networks |
| VL-74 | **A ONE-PARAMETER compensated 500-neuron subcircuit reproduces the 184,099-neuron full brain's readout to within 5.4% at every validation rate from 40 to 200 Hz, out of sample and inside ACC-3's own tolerance. | VL-90 | **What Gate G3's closure (D-251) does and does not cover: every figure behind it was measured on a QUIET SYNTHETIC NETWORK, on one host, on one day, in single samples — and two days before the network the MVP actually ships existed.** VL-41, VL-42 and VL-43 each say so in their own last sentence. The shipped subcircuit is `srext`, admitted 2026-09-13 by D-205: 501 neurons carrying a **format v1.1 compensating-input table** (D-190, IR-NET-09) that did not exist when G3 was measured on 2026-09-11. That table is not free and is not neutral: Appendix C step 0 adds one binary64 addition per neuron per step, and — more significantly — it exists precisely to raise activity, which is the mechanism VL-42 identified as driving the **super-linear** cost growth that refuted two successive duration estimates (D-134's 2000 ms and D-136's 1500 ms). G3's timings are therefore a **lower bound** on what the shipped network costs, and the direction of the error is unfavourable. **Consequence, carried forward:** NFR-PERF-01 and ACC-6 are **not** established by G3 and must be measured on `srext` itself at Phase E, which is what TX-04 already exists to do. **A second correction, per D-253:** VL-41's own text reads "Against NFR-PERF-01's five-minute bound, only N=250 and N=500 fit". That bound was superseded by D-133, which raised it to ten minutes; under the current bound N=1000 (385 s) also fits, which is what the G3 row states. | VL-96 | **NFR-OBS-01 now names the compiler on ACC-5's row 7, and the row's five fingerprints are unmoved by the change — but the platform half of that manifest line is an inference, not a measurement.** Measured 2026-09-15 on TK5 MVS 3.8j under Hercules 4.9.1.11612-SDL-gee86c4de, **JCC 1.50.00**, **SOFT2C**, codepage 819/1047, network `srext` from the installed dataset `HERC01.ONFLY.ENET` (D-286). `python tools/mvsjcc.py --ccprobe` reports that of nineteen candidates JCC predefines exactly two, `JCC` = `1` and `__STDC__` = `1`, and none of `__MVS__`, `__GNUC__`, `__IBMC__`, `__370__`, `__EBCDIC__` or any version macro. `python tools/mvsjcc.py --run --out data/phase-e/jcc` then ran all 22 steps at `COND CODE 0000` in 940.9 s and its manifest reads `ONF002I   COMPILER        JCC` and `ONF002I   PLATFORM        MVS38J`, where before D-291 both read `UNKNOWN`; `--compare data/phase-e/jcc data/phase-d/x86w` gives `ACC-5 row 7 PASS` on G-15 `6C3F7272`, G-16 `BAF81D91`, G-17 `F9C7EE77`, G-18 `4FD0ED1E`, G-19 `C4C320BC`. **Three limits carry forward.** (1) `ONF_PLATID` is derived from a **compiler** macro because JCC predefines no platform macro at all; it is correct for every build ONFLY performs and would print a falsehood for a JCC build aimed at any other target. (2) The manifest carries no compiler **version** — JCC exposes none to the preprocessor, and `1.50.00` is known only from Gate G0's inventory (A-04) and from the comment JCC writes into its generated assembler. (3) Neutrality of the two new branches was measured on **x86-64 only** (gcc 6, Windows: `CCID=GCC PLATID=WIN32` both with and without `-DJCC=1`); that GCCMVS and s390x are unaffected follows from the branch ordering and from `__GNUC__` being defined on both, not from a build run in that session. The 940.9 s is wall clock on a host that was concurrently building TestFloat and is **not** an NFR-PERF-01 or ACC-6 figure |

| VL-100 | **PARTLY SUPERSEDED 2026-09-16 by VL-103 — the verdict stands, the mechanism was wrong.** VL-103 repeats this probe at 30 seeds and finds the 40 Hz column **cleanly monotonic**, so the non-monotonic noise-dominated behaviour recorded below, and the reading built on it that 40 Hz is insensitive to W_syn, are **artefacts of four seeds**. The conclusion that no W_syn passes is confirmed, by a different and cleaner argument. Read VL-103 for the finding and this row only for what four seeds showed. **VL-65's open question is answered: no W_syn lets the dorsal_tpGRN variant pass ACC-4, and the 40 Hz mismatch is not a calibration artefact.** Measured 2026-09-15 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc, **NATIVE** backend, `python prep/wsens.py --variant tpgrn --w-syn 0.26,0.28,0.2969,0.32 --rates 40,60,120 --seeds 4 --jobs 10`, 48 full-brain runs, **3,086 s**. MN9 mean ± standard error (Hz): **W_syn 0.2600** — 40 Hz 3.88 ± 0.90, 60 Hz 9.75 ± 1.45, 120 Hz 29.38 ± 1.68; **0.2800** — 8.50 ± 3.03, 18.62 ± 3.54, 38.50 ± 1.10; **0.2969** — 7.00 ± 2.53, 24.75 ± 3.46, 47.75 ± 1.20; **0.3200** — 8.75 ± 4.61, 32.00 ± 2.10, 63.75 ± 2.13. **The argument.** ACC-4 needs 40 Hz ≤ 6.73, 60 Hz ≥ 24.05 and 120 Hz ≥ 48.32. Interpolating the 60 Hz column between the 0.2800 and 0.2969 rows puts its floor at W_syn ≈ **0.295**, so the feasible window is W_syn ≥ 0.295 — and across it 40 Hz measures 7.0 to 7.8, never reaching 6.73. The one probed value that does pull 40 Hz down, 0.2600 at 3.88 Hz, collapses 60 Hz to 9.75 and 120 Hz to 29.38, far below both floors. **The mechanism is the opposite of what was predicted.** The free screen that preceded this reasoned from a drive-strength proxy that 40 Hz would be the most W_syn-sensitive rate; measurement says 60 and 120 Hz are, at roughly 4 to 5% of response per 1% of W_syn and cleanly monotonic, while 40 Hz is **non-monotonic and noise-dominated** (3.88, 8.50, 7.00, 8.75 with standard errors to 4.61). **This supports risk R-03's reading**: the low-rate mismatch is a structural property of the MaleCNS network, not a constant that calibration can move, which is the evidence D-177 decided without. **Cross-check:** the probe's 0.2969 row agrees with VL-65's 30-seed tpgrn values (7.78, 24.55, 49.10) within sampling error. **Not proven:** four seeds per point, against ACC-4's thirty (D-135), so the 40 Hz column's noise is real and the claim that 40 Hz is insensitive to W_syn is supported, not established at ACC-4 rigour; four W_syn points, not a search, so no recalibrated W_syn was produced; the `right` variant was not probed; x86-64 NATIVE only; and **nothing shipped was touched** — the probe writes only `data/calibration/wsens-tpgrn-s4.json`. *(Renamed from `wsens-tpgrn.json` the same day: the tool named its output by variant alone, so a later run at a different seed count would have silently replaced this evidence — the D-209 failure mode. The seed count is now part of the filename.)* |

COND CODE 0008`, `IEF404I ONFPRUN - ENDED - TIME=16.59.38`, 10,487 lines — and `--recover` retrieved all fourteen records. **COND CODE 0008 is the correct outcome, not a failure:** G-11, G-12 and G-13 are the ONF201W, ONF203E and ONF202E cases and IR-JCL-04 turns them into a step code of 8, exactly as D-273 amended FR-BAT-06 to say; the run reports `ONF302I STEP SUMMARY: 11 OK, 1 WARN, 2 ERROR`. `--compare data/phase-e/mvs data/phase-d/x86w` gives `TX-01 PASS, ACC-5 row 6 PASS` on G-01 `6C143127` through G-14 `8EC69ADE`, all fourteen matching Section 8.4. | VL-102 | **ACC-6 PASS, certified on a clock outside the guest as well as on the guest's own accounting.** Measured 2026-09-15 on TK5 MVS 3.8j under Hercules 4.9.1.11612-SDL-gee86c4de, host CPU 13th Gen Intel Core i7-13650HX, **GCCMVS 3.2.3 at `-O1`**, **SOFT2C**, network `srext`: `python tools/mvsrun.py --buzz --tx04`, one SUGR request at 200 Hz, 1000 ms, seed 1. NFR-PERF-02 context: highest observed MIPS since the last query 471.955. **Why the external check was made.** `tools/mvsrun.py`'s ACC-6 path reads only guest clocks — `IEF374I ... CPU` and the step's `elapsed time` — and `tools/mvsprf.py`'s `trust()` records that **both inflate together through a host sleep**, a contaminated run having reported `CPU 61MIN 21.92SEC` against 61 min 32 s elapsed, ratio 1.00, with ~38 of those minutes in Modern Standby. `mvsrun.py` has no equivalent guard, so one was applied by hand under D-308: over the run's window the Windows power log records **no Kernel-Power transition at all**, and the Hercules *process* consumed **188.29 s of host CPU against 220.3 s of host wall clock**, a ratio of 0.855 once the ~35 s of SCRATCH/STEP1/STEP3 and submit polling are allowed for. Guest 184.67 s and host 188.29 s agree. **Not proven:** TK5 under Hercules on this host only — VL-04 stands, a Hercules figure says nothing about real IBM Z; one request, one sample, so NFR-PERF-01 is shown met at this point and not characterised as a distribution; and the external check is a **floor**, detecting a host that stopped, not one that was merely slow |
| VL-103 | **No W_syn satisfies ACC-4 for the dorsal_tpGRN variant — established at ACC-4's own seed rigour, and for a different reason than VL-100 gave.** Measured 2026-09-15/16 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc, **NATIVE** backend, `python prep/wsens.py --variant tpgrn --w-syn 0.26,0.28,0.2969,0.32 --rates 40,60,120 --seeds 30 --jobs 16`, **360 full-brain runs, 11,047 s**, written to `data/calibration/wsens-tpgrn-s30.json`. MN9 mean ± standard error (Hz): **0.2600** — 40 Hz 5.60 ± 0.50, 60 Hz 10.28 ± 0.77, 120 Hz 28.80 ± 0.62; **0.2800** — 6.97 ± 0.63, 17.53 ± 0.86, 38.42 ± 0.70; **0.2969** — 7.78 ± 0.78, 24.55 ± 1.24, 49.10 ± 1.00; **0.3200** — 11.82 ± 1.44, 34.37 ± 1.16, 63.02 ± 1.22. **The argument, and it is now a clean one.** Every rate rises monotonically with W_syn, so each ACC-4 band becomes a one-sided constraint: 40 Hz is above its band at the shipped W_syn (7.78 > 6.73) and needs **W_syn ≤ 0.2766**; 60 Hz is only just inside its band (24.55 in [24.05, 40.08]) and needs **W_syn ≥ 0.2957**; 120 Hz likewise (49.10 in [48.32, 80.54]) needs **W_syn ≥ 0.2957**. The constraints are **disjoint by 0.0191 mV, 6.9% of W_syn**, so the feasible window is empty. **What that means scientifically:** the failure is not a mis-set gain. W_syn is a single scalar and the variant's dose-response curve has the wrong *shape* against Shiu's — too much response at 40 Hz for the amount it delivers at 60 and 120 Hz — and no scalar can change a shape. **This is the evidence risk R-03 asserts and D-177 decided without.** **It also corrects VL-100.** At four seeds the 40 Hz column read 3.88, 8.50, 7.00, 8.75 and looked non-monotonic; at thirty it reads 5.60, 6.97, 7.78, 11.82 and is monotonic. VL-100's verdict was right and its mechanism was wrong, which is why D-307 declined to settle ACC-4's disposition on it. **Cross-check:** the 0.2969 row reads 7.78 / 24.55 / 49.10, **identical to VL-65's independently measured 30-seed values**. **Not proven:** four W_syn points with linear interpolation between them, not a search — the crossings 0.2766 and 0.2957 are interpolated, and VL-79 measured this model swinging sharply below the fourth decimal, so they locate the window rather than bound it to that precision; the disjointness, at 6.9%, is far larger than that uncertainty. The `right` variant was not probed. x86-64 NATIVE only. **Nothing shipped was touched** |

| VL-107 | *(Annotated 2026-09-16, in the style this register uses for VL-100: the closing "Not proven" clause below — `engine/src/onfker.c` has no chunked entry point and none was added — was true of THIS measurement and stopped being true of the tree later the same day. D-366 added `onfinit` and `onfcont`, and **VL-115 is the engine half**. Nothing else in this entry has changed.)* **A run divided into chunks produces the same answer as one that is not — proved at the oracle level, with the plausible wrong driver shown to be caught.** Measured 2026-09-16 on x86-64 Windows 11, CPython 3.13, **no float backend involved** (the oracle is plain Python floats, Section 8.2). `python tests/test_chunk.py`: **16 tests, OK**. Stage 1, which changes nothing: the xorshift32 stream is identical whether drawn whole or in chunks of K ∈ {1, 2, 3, 7, 10, 100, 997} for seeds 0, 1, 7, 12345 and 999999999, including the D-32 seed-0 remap; the stimulus draws layered on it likewise, with the rejection path shown to fire and to consume a **variable** number of u32 per step; and `fingerprint()` shown to depend on total steps and on accumulated counts, so a chunked run must report totals rather than a last chunk's. Stage 2, against `kernel.run()`: `run_chunked()` equals `run()` for every K ∈ {1, 2, 17, 18, 19, 100, 900, 905} at seeds 0, 1 and 7, on a fixture whose spikes propagate round a ring so the delay ring and the IR-NET-06 accumulation order are exercised, and again with a v1.1 compensating table. **K = 17, 18 and 19 bracket the delay of 18 deliberately**, that being where a ring-phase error would surface first. **The negative case is what makes it evidence:** a driver restarting `t` at zero each chunk — the natural mistake, since the body uses `t` for the arrival slot, the emission slot and the first-spike latency — produces a different answer and is asserted to. **The refactor that made this testable is proved neutral:** `oracle/onfly_oracle/kernel.py` gained `_State`, `_init()`, `_advance()` and `run_chunked()`, and the `git diff` removes only `def run(...)`, its docstring, `for t in range(steps):` and `return spikes, first` — **not one line of the normative loop body**. `mingw32-make kernel golden` then gives `run_ker: 449 passed, 0 failed` and `run_gld: 20 passed, 0 failed` on **SOFT3E, NATIVE and SOFT2C**, with `cmpgld: SOFT3E, NATIVE, SOFT2C agree on all 19 golden requests across 2 networks`. **Not proven:** this is the **oracle**, not the engine — `engine/src/onfker.c` has no chunked entry point and none was added, so what is established is that the *algorithm* carries no hidden per-call state, not that Phase G's C driver will carry it correctly; that is what FR-SIM-10 (P-15) would oblige and what the Phase G test must show against the golden suite. x86-64 CPython only; nothing was run on MVS or s390x |
| VL-108 | **ACC-4's failure is structural on the configuration ACC-4 is actually evaluated on: no W_syn satisfies it for the shipped D-52 stimulus set, and 40 Hz is outside tolerance at every W_syn probed.** Measured 2026-09-16 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc, **NATIVE** backend, `python prep/wsens.py --w-syn 0.26,0.28,0.2969,0.32 --rates 10,40,60,120 --seeds 30 --jobs 16`, **480 full-brain runs, 14,757 s**, written to `data/calibration/wsens-d52-s30.json`. The finding D-177 recorded and D-310 chose to carry is now evidenced on the shipped model, not only on a variant. **Not proven:** four W_syn points with linear interpolation, not a search — the crossings locate the window rather than bound it to four decimals, and VL-79 measured this model swinging sharply below the fourth decimal; the disjointness at 11.4% is far larger than that uncertainty. 200 Hz was not probed. x86-64 NATIVE only. **Nothing shipped was touched** |

RC= 8` with `ONF202E`, where the previous run printed `RATE= 1` for a request the engine had correctly rejected as −1. **The sign is present; its placement is awkward** — `PIC -ZZZ9` is a *fixed* insertion sign, occupying column one while `ZZZ9` right-justifies the digit, so −1 renders `-   1` rather than `   -1`. Clock certified: `4840.8 s over 4852.4 s elapsed => 100% of one core`. **Why the picture changed.** `PIC -ZZZ9` is a **fixed** insertion sign — column one, while `ZZZ9` right-justifies the digit. `PIC ----9` floats the sign to the digit and is the same five columns. **The more useful half is the test.** `CLEAN_CARDS` had fed `  -1` to the card **parser** since D-265, so the sign was checked going in; nothing fed a negative rate to the **report**, so when `RL-RATE` dropped it coming out, no x86 test could see it. A seventh synthetic response makes `RATE=   -1` a proxy assertion and a negative card now checks the 412-byte packing. **Its teeth were verified rather than assumed:** restoring `PIC -ZZZ9` makes `tests/run_cob.py` print `RATE=-   1` against the expected `RATE=   -1` and fail at exit 2; `PIC ----9` passes at exit 0. **Not proven:** TK5 under Hercules only (VL-04); MVT COBOL, not Enterprise COBOL, so VL-02 stands; one run |

The same 150 full-brain runs, the same per-rate means and standard errors, the same reference. `--reeval` exists so the verdict is printed by the tool under the amended rule rather than a reader being asked to reinterpret the old `FAIL`, and its output names the file it read and the platform that produced it. **What this result is NOT.** It is not evidence that ONFLY reproduces Shiu's magnitudes — it does not, at 10 and 40 Hz, by +3.67 and +9.62 Hz, and VL-103, VL-108 and VL-109 show no W_syn fixes that for any stimulus set considered. **The bar was lowered by D-341**, on VL-06's grounds that a male connectome against a female-calibrated model shows consistency rather than identity. | VL-113 | **TBD-06's seed count is chosen on measurement, and the measurement is x86-only.** Measured 2026-09-16 on x86-64 Windows 11, mingw32 gcc 6.3.0, **NATIVE** backend (`build/runnet.exe`, `-DONF_FP_NATIVE`), shipped `srext` network: `python prep/seeds.py --seeds 200 --jobs 14` -- 1,000 engine runs in 312 s -- then `python prep/seeds.py --reeval`; raw data and analysis in `data/calibration/seeds.json`. **The sweep is the same engine that produced the acceptance record:** seeds 1..30 reproduce `acc1-candidate.json` (VL-105) exactly, the same means to within 1e-12 and the same count of seeds that spiked at every rate, and the tool stops rather than analyse if they do not. **Measured, non-parametric** (bootstrap, B = 10,000, resampling the 200 real seeds): ACC-1's clause flips in **0.00%** of resamples at every candidate count from 5 to 100, because every applicable rate has all seeds spiking; ACC-3 re-evaluated against the recorded reference flips 18.13% at n=5, 8.51% at 10, 4.51% at 15, 2.41% at 20, **0.74% at 30**, 0.08% at 50 and 0.00% at 100. **Modelled** (normal theory from `acc4.json`'s recorded means and sds, because no per-seed full-brain values exist -- D-351): ACC-4's shape clause fails with probability 1.46e-03 at n=5 falling to 1.55e-11 at n=30 -- it gets EASIER as the count rises, because the bar sd/sqrt(n) and the drop's sampling error both shrink as n^-0.5 while the true drop does not move. **What this does not prove:** every figure is x86-64 NATIVE on the shipped subcircuit -- nothing here is an MVS, s390x or soft-backend result; it measures the SAMPLING VARIABILITY of the verdicts, not whether the model matches the fly, which is ACC-3's and ACC-4's separate business; and the ACC-4 figures rest on normality, which is a poor fit at 10 Hz. |
| VL-114 | **Two sensitivities that 30 seeds does not remove, recorded as findings under D-353 rather than resolved.** Derived 2026-09-16 from VL-113's measurement, same platform and artefacts. **(1) ACC-3 is reproducible as a RE-EVALUATION and not as a RE-MEASUREMENT.** `prep/extract.py --acc3-file` reads the full-brain reference from the recorded `acc4.json` and re-runs only the subcircuit, which is stable -- 0.74% flip at 30 seeds. If the full brain were re-run too, the reference moves as well: at 40 Hz its per-seed sd is 8.449 Hz, so its standard error at 30 seeds is 1.5425 Hz against a tolerance band of 1.435 Hz -- **the reference's own sampling error exceeds the whole tolerance.** P(ACC-3 passes a re-seeded campaign, over the four rates it tests) is **52.3% to 60.3% at 30 seeds** and 73.9% to 78.2% at 100, the two ends being correlation 0 (D-351's bound) and correlation +1 between the two halves; reaching 95% would need 441 to 631 seeds, about 28 to 40 hours of full-brain compute on this host by linear scaling of `acc4.json`'s 6,971 s -- an extrapolation, not a measurement. **(2) D-202's 10 Hz exclusion is LOAD-BEARING.** At 10 Hz the subcircuit produces 0.0075 Hz against a reference of 3.6667 Hz and a tolerance of 1.0 Hz, so if that rate were TESTED rather than excluded ACC-3 would fail outright. The classification rests on a measured sd/mean ratio of 1.152; normal theory -- a poor fit at exactly this skewed rate, so indicative rather than calibrated -- puts P(a re-measured campaign does NOT exclude it) at 28.4% for 30 seeds and 14.8% for 100. More seeds **resolves** that classification rather than protecting it: whichever side of 1 the population ratio lies, a larger count makes the answer certain, and a seed count cannot choose which side. **Neither finding is a regression.** ACC-3's recorded PASS (VL-98) stands on the campaign that was actually run; what is now on the record is its precision. |
